*Sticheron 1:* Walking in the footsteps of Christ's passions, the whole assembly of martyrs went steadfastly unto many contests, and preached Him as God before ungodly tormentors and lawless kings, and endured many torments, expecting to receive heavenly honors; seeing which they now rejoice, and stand before the Lord with all the choirs of bodiless powers.[^7744]

*Sticheron 2:* A God-chosen flock, imitators of Christ the Chief Shepherd were ye, O all-sacred shepherds: ye preserved unharmed unto the end the divine treasures of piety, having driven away the wild wolves, and guided it well into the heavenly fold; wherein being now established, remember with love those who praise you, and with boldness pray unto Christ for our souls.[^7745]

*Sticheron 3:* Having lived venerably, all ye venerable fathers, ye conquered the demons, and having quenched the burning consciences of the torment of passions, ye valiantly endured, O Blessed Ones; and ye now rejoice with the heavenly powers, for in the flesh ye were likened unto the life of those bodiless ones; with whom pray Christ, the most good God, to find deliverance from falls...[^7746]

...unto those who honor you.[^7747]

*Other Stichera to the Martyrs, Tone and Podoben the same:*[^7748]

*Sticheron 1:* Thy martyrs, O Lord, denied Thee not, neither did they depart from Thy commandments. By their prayers have mercy upon us. *(Twice)*[^7749]

*Sticheron 2:* Passion-bearing martyrs, citizens of heaven, having suffered upon earth, endured many torments. By their prayers, O Lord, and by their entreaty, preserve us all.[^7750]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion (Dogmatikon):* Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who will not hymn thy birthgiving without defilement? For the Only-Begotten Son, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the same came forth from thee, O Pure One, having become ineffably incarnate: being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake: not divided into two persons, but known unconfusedly in two natures. Him do thou entreat, O pure and all-blessed one, that our souls may be saved.[^7751]

*O Joyful Light. Prokeimenon of the day.*[^7752]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera to the Martyrs, Tone 6:*[^7753]

*Sticheron 1:* Those who suffered for Thy sake, O Christ, endured many torments, and received perfect crowns in the heavens: that they may pray for our souls.[^7754]

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... so our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have mercy on us.*[^7755]

*Sticheron 2:* Thy Cross, O Lord, became an unconquerable weapon unto the martyrs; for beholding impending death and foreseeing future life, they were strengthened by their hope in Thee; by their prayers have mercy upon us.[^7756]

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... Our soul is exceedingly filled with the reproach of the prosperous, and with the contempt of the proud.*[^7757]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Thy creative command was my beginning and foundation; for desiring to fashion me as a living creature out of invisible and visible nature, Thou didst fashion my body from the earth, and didst give me a soul by Thy divine and life-creating breathing. Therefore, O Savior, give rest unto Thy servants in the land of the living, and in the tents of the righteous.[^7758]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* By the prayers of her who gave Thee birth, O Christ, of Thy martyrs, and apostles, and prophets, and hierarchs, venerable ones, and righteous...[^7759]

...righteous, and of all the saints, give rest unto Thy departed servants.[^7760]

*On Friday at Compline.*[^7761]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*[^7762]

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:* He was my helper and protector unto salvation; this is my God, and I will glorify Him; the God of my father, and I will exalt Him; for gloriously is He glorified.[^7763]

*Refrain: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*[^7764]

*Troparion 1:* Cast me not away, O Good One, abhor me not, nor despise me, who have fervently fled beneath thy tender compassion, O Pure One; but grant me to be nourished in thee by grace.[^7765]

*Troparion 2:* O good Theotokos, helper of the sorrowing, receive the sighing from my soul, and deliver me from all the immeasurable evils which I have cruelly committed.[^7766]

*Glory:* Unto thee I fall down, O Good One, intercessor for the sorrowful: deliver me from the eternal fire, and the darkness, and the abyss, who have lived evilly throughout my whole life.[^7767]

*Both now:* Woe is me! How shall I entreat Thee, O Deliverer, my Jesus, having sinned immeasurably against Thee? Therefore I bring unto Thee as intercessor the Pure One who gave Thee birth: have mercy and save me.[^7768]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish, O Lord, upon the rock of Thy commandments my shaken heart, for Thou alone art holy and Lord.[^7769]

*Troparion 1:* I fall down before thee, O Mother of the Word: receive me with thy compassions; and grant forgiveness of transgressions unto me who ask, by thy fervent prayers.[^7770]

*Troparion 2:* Have mercy on me, O Lady, have mercy, and deliver me from every malice, and the affliction of demons, and eternal torment.[^7771]

*Glory:* Subdue, O Lady, the bitter drunkenness of my pleasures, granting me the divine sobriety of repentance and saving conversion.[^7772]

*Both now:* O Parent of God, who ineffably gavest birth unto the Lord and Ruler of all, entreat Him that He may save thy flock.[^7773]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid, that Thou wast about to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: I heard the report of Thee, and was afraid; glory to Thy power, O Lord.[^7774]

*Troparion 1:* Ceaselessly do I scratch the wounds of my soul with love of pleasure, and the pains of ulcers have been added unto me...[^7775]

...pains, and I remain insensible and incurable; have mercy, O Parent of God, and heal and save me by thy prayers.[^7776]

*Troparion 2:* The darkness of sin hath covered my soul, O Parent of God, so that I walk as in night in the light, ignorant of Christ's commandments; O thou who gavest birth to the Divine Light, have mercy on me and enlighten me, I pray.[^7777]

*Glory:* The Word of the living God came down into thy womb, O Virgin-Mother, and taking from thy most pure blood my whole nature, He comes twofold in one Hypostasis; entreat Him to save our souls.[^7778]

*Both now:* Diligently have I pursued bodily desires and all pleasures, and I am wholly corrupted, defiled, and abominable; O most pure Parent of God, have mercy and save me by thy tender compassion.[^7779]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Enlighten, I pray, him who rises early from the night, O Lover of mankind, and guide me unto Thy commandments, and teach me, O Savior, to do Thy will.[^7780]

*Troparion 1:* I have kindled for myself the fire of Gehenna, doing unfitting deeds, and have cruelly drawn upon myself the wrath of God; help me, O Pure One, and forsake me not.[^7781]

*Troparion 2:* Ceaselessly do I ask remission of transgressions, yet I cease not committing most lawless deeds, and I grieve thee, O Most Pure One; O cleansing of the faithful, have compassion on me.[^7782]

*Glory:* Whom thou hast borne from thy blood, O Pure One, perfect man and true God, Jesus: entreat Him that we may be delivered from eternal fire.[^7783]

*Both now:* O Impassable Gate, I pray: open unto me the doors of true repentance, and show me the paths of repentance, O Pure One, who art the guide of all.[^7784]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I cried with all my heart unto the merciful God, and He heard me from the lowest hades, and brought up my life from corruption.[^7785]

*Troparion 1:* The waves of improper thoughts now submerge me; but do thou, O Lady, guide me by thy tender compassion unto the calm haven of true repentance.[^7786]

*Troparion 2:* Having acquired thee as hope and unconquerable wall, and a firm protectress, O Maiden...[^7787]

...we are delivered from transgressions, and cruel passions, and every harm.[^7788]

*Glory:* I fall down before thee, O pure Virgin, and cry aloud with weeping, O Theotokos: deliver my wretched soul from the future judgment and the eternal fire.[^7789]

*Both now:* Thou art the strength, O Most Pure One, of those infirm in soul through thy supplications; therefore despise me not who am sick in soul, but heal me.[^7790]

*Then, Lord, have mercy (40 times). Glory... both now...*[^7791]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 6:* For the tender compassion of Thy mercies, O Christ, Thou camest down to earth, and taking flesh of the Virgin, Thou didst sanctify all, and didst call all from earth to heaven; therefore, hoping in Thee, we shall never err, but are delivered by Thee from present assaults; for Thou art our Savior, O our Creator God.[^7792]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* We have sinned, we have transgressed, we have done unrighteously before Thee, neither have we kept nor done as Thou hast commanded us; but deliver us not up utterly, O God of the fathers.[^7793]

*Troparion 1:* When my wretched soul must endure separation from the body, and there will be none to deliver it or comfort it: then stand forth, O Lady, and deliver me from the malice of demons.[^7794]

*Troparion 2:* I fall down before thee, and offer fervent drops of tears; I know thy love for mankind, I know thy longsuffering and meekness: do thou now have mercy on me, O Pure One, pardon and save me.[^7795]

*Glory:* Have mercy, O Virgin, upon my passionate and wretched soul; see the turmoil of passions, see also the inconstant burning of the flesh; and grant me saving help and deliverance.[^7796]

*Both now:* The Son and Word, consubstantial and co-unoriginate with the Father, took flesh from thee, equal, endowed with mind and soul, unchangeably, as He Himself knows; and He renewed our nature therein for the better, O pure Virgin.[^7797]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and Cherubim and Seraphim tremble before: let every breath and creation sing, bless, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^7798]

*Troparion 1:* I am at a loss pondering my deeds, and I tremble before the Judge of the dread tribunal; what defense shall I, the wretched one, then offer? O Lady...[^7799]

...Lady of the world, be thou my protectress.[^7800]

*Troparion 2:* Do not thou thyself, O Lady, cast me away from thy face when I look upon thine icon; but be merciful unto me, and alter my present condemnation.[^7801]

*Glory:* O Bride of God, unwedded Mary! Deliver me from every wicked harm as I, thy servant, cry unto thee; and stand by me in the future trial, O only protectress of Christians.[^7802]

*Both now:* That Thy Son might deify mankind, O Lady, He appeared from thee as perfect man; entreat Him therefore to show me, perfectly cleansed, a partaker of His divine kingdom.[^7803]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* O Virgin who didst receive from the Angel the "Rejoice," and gavest birth to thy Creator: save those who magnify thee.[^7804]

*Troparion 1:* Thou alone art the protectress of mankind, O Most Pure One! Thou art the wall of Christians, O Pure One! Thee do I now present as an intercessor unto Christ for me the humble one, that by thy prayers He may have mercy on me the wretched.[^7805]

*Troparion 2:* I have wrought the works of night, and the night of torment is now about to cover me justly, convicted, and to receive all torment of hades; but O pure Virgin who didst bear the Judge and God, deliver me from every torment.[^7806]

*Glory:* I have spent the time of my life in evils, and have drawn near unto the gates of hades, and all unprepared do I go thither. Help me, O good Theotokos, for in thee have I placed my hope.[^7807]

*Both now:* O good-loving Lady, adornment of Angels, glory of Martyrs: with these pray that we may find mercy and remission of debts, and that all may be able rightly to finish the course of our life, doing divine things.[^7808]

*It is truly meet. Trisagion. After the Our Father: troparia and the rest as usual.*[^7809]

*On Saturday at Matins.*[^7810]

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymns to the Martyrs, Tone 6:*[^7811]

*Sessional Hymn 1:* Thou art ever a light unto the righteous, O Lord! For the saints, having been enlightened in Thee, shine ever as luminaries...[^7812]

...having quenched the lamp of the ungodly. By their prayers, O our Savior, enlighten Thou my lamp, and save me.[^7813]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^7814]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* Having endured the struggle of suffering, O saints, and received from Thee the honors of victory, they brought to nought the devices of the lawless, and received incorruptible crowns. Being entreated by them, O God, grant us great mercy.[^7815]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Thou Who didst call Thy Mother blessed, camest to Thy passion by voluntary counsel, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the Angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost coin is found. O our God Who hast ordered all things wisely, glory to Thee.[^7816]

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymns to the Martyrs, Tone 6:*[^7817]

*Sessional Hymn 1:* The suffering standing in the contest, and the wounds of torment upon the martyrs: and the choirs of the bodiless stood holding the honors of victory: the all-wise ones amazed tormentors and kings; they cast down the apostate by the confession of Christ. O Lord Who didst strengthen them, glory to Thee.[^7818]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^7819]

*Sessional Hymn 2:* O Lord, the memory of Thy saints today is shown forth as the Paradise that was in Eden; for therein all creation rejoices, and by their supplications Thou grantest us peace and great mercy.[^7820]

*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord will deliver them.*[^7821]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Truly all things are vanity, and life is but a shadow and a dream; for in vain doth everyone born of earth disquiet himself, as the Scripture saith: when we have acquired the world, then do we dwell in the grave, where kings and paupers are together. Therefore, O Christ God, give rest unto the departed, as the Lover of mankind.[^7822]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "O hope of the world" (Наде́жде мі́ра):*[^7823] O defense of those born of earth, protectress Mary, who didst bear in time the timeless Son and Word of God: entreat Him earnestly, as His Mother, with the martyrs, and prophets, and divine fasters, to save those who have departed.[^7824]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and the Departed.*[^7825]

*(Whose acrostic is: With words I honor the lovers of the Word.) A work of Joseph.*[^7826]

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:* The sensory Pharaoh was drowned with all his host, but Israel, having passed through the midst of the sea, cried aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord our God, for gloriously is He glorified.[^7827]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^7828]

*Troparion 1:* With radiant mind ye enlightened those in darkness, and having put to shame the impious tormentors, O Martyrs, being clearly victorious, ye passed over unto the unwaning light.[^7829]

*Refrain: Hierarchs of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^7830]

*Troparion 2:* The hierarchs of Christ, and the choir of the venerable, and of the prophets, and the unique solemnity of all the righteous, shining with virtuous beauties, have entered into the heavenly mansions.[^7831]

*Refrain: All Saints, pray to God for us.*[^7832]

*Troparion 3:* The whole multitude of women, having betrothed themselves unto Christ and trampled underfoot with manly labors him who slew Eve by cruel deceit, are called blessed with divine songs.[^7833]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Thou Who from the earth didst create man in the beginning, O Christ: give rest to the souls of Thy servants, we pray, in the mansions of the righteous and in places of refreshment, as Thou art most good.[^7834]

*Both now:* More sacred than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O Pure One, wast thou revealed, having given birth to the Creator of creation; entreat Him unceasingly to have compassion upon thy servants who glorify thee.[^7835]

*Another Canon to the Departed.*[^7836]

*(Whose acrostic is: A sixth song I sing for the departed.) We sing it in order where there is no Menaion. A work of Theophanes.*[^7837]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh their pursuer drowning, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God.[^7838]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^7839]

*Troparion 1:* In the heavenly chambers the noble martyrs ever pray Thee, O Christ: vouchsafe the faithful whom Thou hast removed from the earth to obtain eternal blessings.[^7840]

*Verse: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^7841]

*Troparion 2:* Having adorned all things, Thou didst create between them man, a mixed creature, both of humility and greatness; therefore give rest, O Savior, to the souls of Thy servants.[^7842]

*Glory:* In the beginning Thou didst establish me as a dweller and worker of Paradise...[^7843]

...yet when I transgressed Thy commandment, Thou didst drive me out; therefore give rest, O Savior, to the souls of Thy servants.[^7844]

*Both now:* He Who from a rib created of old Eve our first mother, is clothed in flesh from thy most pure womb, whereby, O Pure One, He hath destroyed the power of death.[^7845]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Having established the thoughts of my soul upon the firm rock of Thy faith, establish me, O Lord; for I have Thee, O Good One, as refuge and affirmation.[^7846]

*Troparion 1:* Blended with bodily pains, the sufferers looked rejoicing toward the painless recompense; and now they soothe our many pains by grace.[^7847]

*Troparion 2:* Driving away evil beasts with a firm understanding, the divine hierarchs delivered unharmed from their malice the divine nurslings of Christ.[^7848]

*Troparion 3:* The choir of Venerables, voluntarily submitting beneath the yoke of the Lord, mortified carnal wisdom, and received eternal life.[^7849]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Deliver from eternal fire those who in piety have departed from this life, O Christ, and grant remission of their debts, O Good One, and eternal delight.[^7850]

*Both now:* The women who loved Christ, standing about thee who didst ineffably give Him birth, O most pure Lady, dance in chorus with joyful mind.[^7851]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^7852]

*Troparion 1:* Having lawfully suffered, Thy martyrs, O Giver of life, adorned with crowns of victory from Thee, earnestly intercede for eternal honor for the departed faithful.[^7853]

*Troparion 2:* Having chastened me who had gone astray of old with many wonders and signs, at last Thou didst empty Thyself, being compassionate; and having sought, Thou didst find and save me.[^7854]

*Glory:* Vouchsafe those who have passed over unto Thee from the fleeting, inconstant corruption, to dwell with joy in eternal habitations, O Good One, having justified them by faith and grace.[^7855]

*Both now:* There is none blameless like thee, O most pure Mother of God; for thou alone...[^7856]

...didst conceive in thy womb the true God before the ages, Who destroyed the power of death.[^7857]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, the report of Thee, and was afraid; I understood Thy works, and was filled with awe: glory to Thy power, O Lord.[^7858]

*Troparion 1:* Standing, the passion-bearers beheld the proud one trampled under their feet, and thankfully they glorified the Creator of all.[^7859]

*Troparion 2:* Armed with the splendors of words unto the light of understanding, the hierarchs saved men from the darkness of heresies.[^7860]

*Troparion 3:* Appearing as burning coals through fervent understanding toward God, and having burned up material passions, the Venerable are greatly glorified.[^7861]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Ruling over both the living and the dead, O Lord: give rest to those whom Thou hast removed, with all who have well-pleased Thee, O Master.[^7862]

*Both now:* The Lord, O Most Pure One, having ineffably clothed Himself in flesh from thee, received the company of women who contended courageously.[^7863]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in God-befitting manner, crying out from a pure mind, feasting in the Lord.[^7864]

*Troparion 1:* Manifesting a greater knowledge of wisdom, and in Thy gifts an all-perfect goodness, O Master, Thou didst unite the choirs of martyrs with the Angels.[^7865]

*Troparion 2:* Vouchsafe the departed who have passed unto Thee to obtain Thine ineffable glory, O Christ, where is the habitation of those who rejoice, and the sound of pure gladness.[^7866]

*Glory:* Receive those singing of Thy divine dominion, whom Thou hast received from the earth: making them children of light, cleansing the mist of sin, O Plenteous in mercy.[^7867]

*Both now:* As a most pure receptacle, a blameless church, an all-holy ark, a virginal place of sanctification: thee, the beauty of Jacob, hath the Master chosen.[^7868]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Christ Who hast shone forth light unto the world: enlighten my heart, as I cry to Thee from the night, and save me.[^7869]

*Troparion 1:* Having clothed yourselves in the garment woven of grace from on high, O sufferers, ye stripped the enemy bare.[^7870]

*Troparion 2:* With the holy prophets let us honor the God-wise high priests, and...[^7871]

...the venerable who have well-pleased God.[^7872]

*Troparion 3:* In psalms and songs let us praise the company of women, as having well-pleased God rightly.[^7873]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* In the habitations of Thy righteous, O Lord, establish Thy servants, overlooking their sins in this life.[^7874]

*Both now:* Delivering us from every harm of the enemy, thou hast appeared, O Lady, praying to Christ the only compassionate One.[^7875]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who rise early unto Thee with love, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, calling them back from the darkness of sin.[^7876]

*Troparion 1:* Offered as a consecrated whole burnt-offering and as the first-fruits of human nature unto God Who is glorified, the Martyrs ever intercede for our salvation.[^7877]

*Troparion 2:* Vouchsafe Thy departed faithful servants heavenly dwelling and the distribution of gifts, O Lord, granting them deliverance from transgressions.[^7878]

*Glory:* O Thou Who alone art the Life-creator by nature, Who art truly an unsearchable abyss of goodness: vouchsafe Thy kingdom unto those who have reposed, O Merciful, only Immortal One.[^7879]

*Both now:* Strength and song is He Who was born of thee unto the world, O Lady, and became salvation unto the lost, delivering from the gates of hades those who bless thee in faith.[^7880]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Swallowed by the whale of sin, I cry unto Thee, O Christ: as the Prophet, deliver me from corruption.[^7881]

*Troparion 1:* By the flow of your divine blood ye drowned the noetic enemies; and ye watered the hearts of the faithful, O Passion-bearers.[^7882]

*Troparion 2:* Having crucified yourselves to the world and passions, O Venerable ones and all-wise Hierarchs, ye were counted worthy of divine glory.[^7883]

*Troparion 3:* As is due, let us call blessed the choir of prophets, and the assembly of honorable women who strove well.[^7884]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Give rest, O God, to the souls of those who have departed before, with Thine elect, overlooking their transgressions.[^7885]

*Both now:* O thou who didst bear Christ in the flesh, mortify the passions of my flesh, and...[^7886]

...quicken my soul, O Virgin, by thine intercession.[^7887]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, having fled to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Plenteous in mercy.[^7888]

*Troparion 1:* Being nailed to the Cross, Thou didst gather unto Thyself the choirs of martyrs who imitated Thy passion, O Good One; therefore we pray Thee: give rest to those who have departed unto Thee.[^7889]

*Troparion 2:* When Thou comest in Thine ineffable glory dreadfully to judge the whole world, be well pleased, O Deliverer, that the faithful servants whom Thou hast taken from the earth may meet Thee radiantly upon the clouds.[^7890]

*Glory:* Being the Fountain of life, O Master, in divine strength bringing out the bound, settle Thy servants who have departed unto Thee in faith in the delight of Paradise.[^7891]

*Both now:* Unto the earth we returned having transgressed the commandment of God; but for thy sake, O Virgin, we were lifted up from earth to heaven, having shaken off mortal corruption.[^7892]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Thou Who didst hear the song of Thy holy Children and didst bedew the burning furnace: blessed art Thou, O Lord.[^7893]

*Troparion 1:* Let us honor with songs the passion-bearers of Christ, who quenched the flame of cruel godlessness with showers of blood.[^7894]

*Troparion 2:* The all-glorious Hierarchs, having dispelled the winter of heresies, passed rejoicing unto the divine springtime.[^7895]

*Troparion 3:* By endurance ye inherited the rich grace of the Spirit, O Fasters, and destroyed a multitude of demons.[^7896]

*Glory:* Vouchsafe those who in faith have departed from this life to inherit the haven of Thy sweetness, O Merciful One, as Thou art plenteous in mercy.[^7897]

*Both now:* He Who showed thee to be a pure Virgin even after childbirth hath saved the choirs of holy women who followed thee.[^7898]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* The Angel made the furnace dew-yielding for the holy Children; while the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^7899]

*Troparion 1:* Delivered by Thy blood from the first transgression, the Martyrs...[^7900]

...and being sprinkled with their own blood, they clearly prefigure Thy slaying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^7901]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst put to death raging death, O most life-originating Word; but receive in Thy faith those who have reposed, who hymn Thee, O Christ, and say: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^7902]

*Glory:* Having enlivened me, man, with divine inbreathing, O Sovereign Master origin of Godhead: vouchsafe the departed Thy kingdom, to sing unto Thee, O Savior: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^7903]

*Both now:* Thou wast higher than all creatures, O All-immaculate One, having conceived God Who shattered the gates of death and broke the bars; therefore, O Pure One, we the faithful hymn thee as Mother of God.[^7904]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* In the furnace Thy venerable Children imitated the Cherubim, chanting the thrice-holy hymn: Bless, sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^7905]

*Troparion 1:* Valiantly wounded by the surge of tortures, the divine Martyrs, having passed through by grace into deep calm, reached the kingdom on high.[^7906]

*Troparion 2:* The venerable and all-wise Hierarchs, having shone forth like the sun, enlighten the universe with the rays of their teachings and the brightness of their healings.[^7907]

*Troparion 3:* Glorious Prophets and Chief Priests, all venerable and righteous ones, multitudes of martyrs and women: preserve all your flock unshaken by demons.[^7908]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Let us hymn the righteous from of old and the God-speaking Prophets, and let us cry aloud with compunction: By their prayers, O Word, give rest unto those who have reposed in faith.[^7909]

*Both now:* The choir of women loved thee, O Virgin, as neighbor wholly to God, and was brought after thee unto the Master of all, praising thee with one accord, O Most Pure One.[^7910]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* From the flame Thou didst pour out dew for the holy Children, and didst consume with fire the sacrifice of the righteous one and the water; for Thou, O Christ, doest all things by the mere act of willing; Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages.[^7911]

*Troparion 1:* Steadfastly showing forth their struggles, they received crowns of victory, the...[^7912]

...Martyrs, passion-bearers of Christ, crying aloud: Thee, the Lord, do we supremely exalt unto the ages.[^7913]

*Troparion 2:* Receive gently, and give rest as Thou art compassionate, unto the faithful who have holily left earthly things and departed unto Thee, the Master, who supremely exalt Thee unto the ages.[^7914]

*Glory:* Now in the land of the meek, be well pleased, O Savior, that all who have reposed before may take up their dwelling, having justified them through faith in Thee and by grace, who supremely exalt Thee unto the ages.[^7915]

*Both now:* We all call thee blessed, O All-blessed One, as having given birth unto the Word truly existing and blessed, Who became flesh for our sake, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.[^7916]

*My soul doth magnify the Lord.*[^7917]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* O Virgin who didst receive from the Angel the "Rejoice," and gavest birth to thy Creator: save those who magnify thee.[^7918]

*Troparion 1:* As pure sheep ye were brought unto the Master, O Passion-bearing Martyrs: pray Him to save our souls.[^7919]

*Troparion 2:* As pastors upon the pasture of piety, O Hierarchs, ye shepherded the faithful; and now ye have taken up your dwelling in the divine sheepfold.[^7920]

*Troparion 3:* With the holy Hierarchs and Prophets, and with the choir of women who suffered valiantly, let us call blessed the choirs of the venerable.[^7921]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* The perpetual joy whereof the assemblies of the Saints partake: vouchsafe the departed to obtain this, O Plenteous in mercy.[^7922]

*Both now:* O Virgin who didst give birth to the Light, enlighten my soul, driving away the darkness of sin and of my slothfulness.[^7923]

*Canon to the Departed. Heirmos:* It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O All-pure One, the Word Incarnate appeared unto men; Whom magnifying with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.[^7924]

*Troparion 1:* Hope strengthened the choirs of the martyrs, and winged them with burning desire unto Thy love, prefiguring for them the truly unshaken rest to come; whereof vouchsafe the departed faithful, O Good One.[^7925]

*Troparion 2:* Be well pleased, O Christ, that those who have reposed in faith may obtain Thy radiant and divine illumination, and the rest in the bosom of Abraham, as Thou alone art merciful unto them...[^7926]

...granting it, and accounting them worthy of eternal blessedness.[^7927]

*Glory:* O Thou Who art by nature good and compassionate, and the Desirer of mercies, and an abyss of loving-kindness: settle in the place where Thy light shines, O Savior, those whom Thou hast translated from this place of affliction and the shadow of death.[^7928]

*Both now:* We know thee, O Pure One, to be the holy Tabernacle, and the Ark, and the Tablets of the Law and of grace; for for thy sake forgiveness was granted unto those justified by the blood of Him Who was incarnate from thy womb, O All-immaculate One.[^7929]

*It is good to give praise: Litany, Exapostilarion, and the usual Psalms.*[^7930]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 6:*

O Lord, at the memory of Thy Saints all creation keeps festival; the heavens rejoice with the Angels, and the earth is glad with men: by their prayers have mercy on us.[^7931]

O Lord, if we had not Thy Saints as intercessors, and Thy goodness showing mercy upon us, how should we dare, O Savior, to hymn Thee, Whom the Angels ceaselessly glorify? O Knower of hearts, spare our souls.[^7932]

The memory of the Martyrs is joy unto those that fear the Lord; for having suffered for Christ's sake, they received crowns from Him; and now with boldness they pray for our souls.[^7933]

Our God is marvelous in His chosen and holy ones. Rejoice and be glad, all ye His servants; for He hath prepared for you crowns and His kingdom. But we pray you, forget us not also.[^7934]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Pain came upon Adam through the tasting of the tree of old in Eden, when the serpent vomited out venom; for on account thereof all-generational death entered in, devouring man; but the Master came and cast down the serpent, and granted us resurrection. Unto Him therefore let us cry aloud: Spare, O Savior, and give rest with the Saints unto those whom Thou hast received, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^7935]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Thou art our God, Who in wisdom createst and fulfillest all things: Thou didst send Prophets, O Christ, to foretell Thy coming; and Apostles, to proclaim Thy majesty. And the former...[^7936]

...indeed prophesied Thy coming, while the latter enlightened the nations by baptism; and the martyrs obtained that which they desired; and the choir of both entreats Thee, together with her that bare Thee: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls which Thou hast received; and vouchsafe us Thy kingdom, O Thou Who didst endure crucifixion for the sake of me the condemned, my Deliverer and God.[^7937]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed, Tone 6.*[^7938]
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

Possessing incomprehensible compassion toward us, and an inexhaustible fountain of divine goodness, O Plenteous in mercy: settle those who have departed unto Thee, O Master, in the land of the living, in the beloved and longed-for dwellings, granting an abiding possession; for Thou didst shed Thy blood for all, O Savior, and didst redeem the world with a life-bearing price.[^7939]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord [LXX Ps. 64:5].*[^7940]

Thou didst willingly endure life-creating putting-to-death, and didst pour out life, and gavest eternal nourishment unto the faithful; wherein station those who have reposed in the hope of resurrection, pardoning all their transgressions by grace, as Thou alone art without sin, and alone art good and the Lover of mankind, that Thy name may be sung by all, O Christ, and Thy saving love for mankind be glorified.[^7941]

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things [LXX Ps. 24:13].*[^7942]

Knowing Thee, O Christ, Who rulest over the living with authority of divine origin, and exercisest lordship over the dead, we pray Thee: give rest unto Thy faithful servants who have departed unto Thee the only Benefactor, with Thine elect, O Lover of mankind, in the place of comfort, in the brightness of the Saints; for Thou art the Desirer of mercy, and as God savest those whom Thou didst create after Thine image, O only Plenteous in mercy.[^7943]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Thou didst appear as a God-befitting dwelling, O Most Pure One; for thou didst contain God, and gavest birth unto Christ, O unwedded Virgin-Mother, in two essences, yet in one Hypostasis. Him do thou entreat, O Pure One, the only-begotten Firstborn, Who preserved thee an immaculate Virgin even after...[^7944]

...childbirth: to give rest in the light and in incorruptible blessedness unto the souls of those who have reposed in faith.[^7945]

*It is good to give praise: Trisagion. After Our Father: Troparion, Litany, and Dismissal.*[^7946]

*On Saturday, The Beatitudes, Tone 6:*

Remember me, O God my Savior, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom, and save me as Thou alone art the Lover of mankind.[^7947]

O Lover of mankind Who didst glorify the prophets and teachers, and the venerable and righteous: by their prayers save our souls.[^7948]

Having not feared the onslaught of fire and sword and wild beasts, O glorious Passion-bearers, ye were accounted worthy of life everlasting.[^7949]

Station with all the saints and righteous ones, O Word, those whom Thou hast translated in faith from temporal things, that we may glorify Thee.[^7950]

*Glory: Triadikon:* O Almighty Trinity, praised in unity! Station in the bosom of Abraham the faithful whom Thou hast translated from the earth.[^7951]

*Both now:* Blessed hast thou appeared in all generations; for thou gavest birth ineffably unto God Who is truly blessed, O Most Pure One.[^7952]

*End of Tone 6.*

*[Engraved Tailpiece: An ornamental baroque vignette in the form of a chalice with floral garlands and arabesques, marking the conclusion of Tone 6]*[^7953]

---

### Notes

[^6519]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Lord I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 1. Sung twice.
[^6520]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Lord I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 2.
[^6521]: Allusion to Psalm 138:7–10 (LXX 138:7–10, 'Whither shall I go from Thy Spirit?').
[^6522]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Lord I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 3.
[^6523]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Lord I have cried, Glory... both now... (Theotokion). A dense Christological dogma asserting the Chalcedonian and Sixth Ecumenical Council definition: two complete natures, two wills, and two natural operations in the one hypostasis of the Word incarnate.
[^6524]: Rubric for the entrance hymn Phos Hilaron, the Saturday evening Prokeimenon (Psalm 92:1), and the evening prayer 'Vouchsafe, O Lord.'
[^6525]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron.
[^6526]: Psalm 44:18 (LXX 44:17).
[^6527]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 1 to the Theotokos (continued across foliation boundary from leaf p626). Melodic model: 'Having laid aside all earthly hope' (Всѐ возло́жше).
[^6528]: Psalm 44:11 (LXX 44:10).
[^6529]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 2 to the Theotokos.
[^6530]: Psalm 44:15–16 (LXX 44:14–15).
[^6531]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 to the Theotokos.
[^6532]: Tone 6 Saturday Little Vespers, Aposticha, Glory... both now... Dogmatic Theotokion. Confirms the hypostatic union of Christ in two unconfused natures against Monophysite and Monothelite heresies.
[^6533]: Rubric governing the conclusion of Little Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion Prayers, Troparion of Tone 6, Theotokion, and dismissal.
[^6534]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 1.
[^6535]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 2.
[^6536]: Allusion to Psalm 138:7–10 (LXX 138:7–10).
[^6537]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Resurrection Sticheron 3.
[^6538]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 4. Allusion to Isaiah 26:13.
[^6539]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 5. Allusion to Hebrews 2:14.
[^6540]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 6.
[^6541]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 7.
[^6542]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Sticheron 8 (first sticheron to the Theotokos by Paul of Amorion, continued across leaf boundary from p628).
[^6543]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Sticheron 9 (second sticheron to the Theotokos by Paul of Amorion).
[^6544]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Sticheron 10 (third sticheron to the Theotokos by Paul of Amorion).
[^6545]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Lord I have cried, Glory... both now... Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 6 (by Saint John Damascene). Classic exposition of the two natures of Christ united without confusion or division in one Person.
[^6546]: Rubric for Phos Hilaron and the Great Prokeimenon 'The Lord is King' (Psalm 92:1).
[^6547]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron.
[^6548]: Psalm 92:1 (LXX 92:1).
[^6549]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 1 (Alphabetical). Allusion to Psalm 106:16.
[^6550]: Psalm 92:1 (LXX 92:1).
[^6551]: Allusion to the lament of the Myrrh-bearing women at the sepulcher (Matthew 28:1–8; Mark 16:1–8; Luke 24:1–10).
[^6552]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 2 (Alphabetical).
[^6553]: Psalm 92:5 (LXX 92:5).
[^6554]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Alphabetical).
[^6555]: Tone 6 Saturday Great Vespers, Aposticha, Glory... both now... (Theotokion). Allusion to the angelic salutation (Luke 1:28).
[^6556]: Resurrectional Troparion of Tone 6 ('The angelic powers were upon Thy tomb').
[^6557]: Allusion to the Parable of the Lost Coin (Drachma), Luke 15:8–10.
[^6558]: Resurrectional Theotokion of Tone 6 ('Calling Thy Mother blessed').
[^6559]: Rubric for the dismissal of Great Vespers.
[^6560]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1 Heirmos. Typology of Israel traversing the Red Sea (Exodus 14:21–31).
[^6561]: Refrain to the Theotokos chanted before each troparion of the canon.
[^6562]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1.
[^6563]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2.
[^6564]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Glory.
[^6565]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6566]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3 Heirmos. Allusion to the Song of Hannah (1 Samuel / 1 Kings 2:1).
[^6567]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 1.
[^6568]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2.
[^6569]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Glory.
[^6570]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6571]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4 Heirmos. Typology of Habakkuk 3:2.
[^6572]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 1.
[^6573]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 2.
[^6574]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Glory.
[^6575]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6576]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5 Heirmos. Allusion to Isaiah 26:9.
[^6577]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1.
[^6578]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2.
[^6579]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Glory. Allusion to Psalm 51:4 (LXX 51:4; 'like a sharp razor').
[^6580]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6581]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6 Heirmos. Typology of the Prophet Jonah (Jonah 2:1–10).
[^6582]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 1.
[^6583]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 2.
[^6584]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Glory.
[^6585]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6586]: Rubric following Ode 6 of Compline Canon.
[^6587]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Sessional Hymn to the Theotokos ('Unashamed protectress of Christians' / 'Предста́тельство Хрⷭ҇тїа́нъ непосты́дное').
[^6588]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7 Heirmos. Typology of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:19–50).
[^6589]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 1. Allusion to Psalm 21:13 (LXX 21:13; 'fat bulls have compassed me').
[^6590]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 2.
[^6591]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Glory.
[^6592]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6593]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8 Heirmos. Typology of the furnace in Babylon and the sacrifice of Elijah on Mount Carmel (1 Kings / 3 Kingdoms 18:38).
[^6594]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 1.
[^6595]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 2.
[^6596]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Glory.
[^6597]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6598]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9 Heirmos. Allusion to the Annunciation (Luke 1:28).
[^6599]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 1.
[^6600]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 2.
[^6601]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Glory.
[^6602]: Tone 6 Saturday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6603]: Rubric concluding the Saturday Compline office.
[^6604]: Rubric opening the Sunday Midnight Office (Въ Недѣ́лю оу҆́трѧ на Полꙋ́нощницѣ).
[^6605]: Title, acrostic, and authorship of the Sunday Midnight Office Canon to the Trinity (composed by Metrophanes of Smyrna, d. c. 910; Greek acrostic: Ἕκτην προσφέρω σοι, Θεότης, ᾠδήν).
[^6606]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 1 Heirmos. Typology of Israel traversing the Red Sea (Exodus 14:21–31).
[^6607]: Refrain to the Most Holy Trinity (Прест҃а́ѧ Трⷪ҇це Бж҃е на́шъ, сла́ва тебѣ̀).
[^6608]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 1, Troparion 1 (Acrostic: Ἕ - Трѝ).
[^6609]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 1, Troparion 2 (Acrostic: κ - Пресꙋ́щный; Slavonic 'кразꙋмѝ' is an archaic/metrical contraction for 'вразомѝ' / 'оу҆кразꙋмѝ').
[^6610]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 1, Glory (Acrostic: τ - Невѣстоѡкраси́въ). Allusion to Romans 11:36 and 2 Corinthians 11:2.
[^6611]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 1, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6612]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 3 Heirmos. Allusion to 1 Samuel / 1 Kings 2:2.
[^6613]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 3, Troparion 1 (Acrostic: η - Оу҆краси́въ).
[^6614]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 3, Troparion 2 (Acrostic: ν - Є҆ди́но).
[^6615]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 3, Glory (Acrostic: π - Оу҆́ме).
[^6616]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 3, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6617]: Rubric following Ode 3 of the Sunday Midnight Office Canon.
[^6618]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Sessional Hymn to the Trinity. Podoben: 'Fulfilling the dispensation for our sake' (Є҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ соверши́въ). Allusion to Romans 8:31 ('If God be for us, who can be against us?').
[^6619]: Conclusion of the Sessional Theotokion from p634, alluding to Isaiah 8:10 and Romans 8:31.
[^6620]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 4 Heirmos. Typology of Habakkuk 3:2.
[^6621]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 4, Troparion 1 (Acrostic: ρ - Возвыша́ѧй).
[^6622]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 4, Troparion 2 (Acrostic: ο - Претворѝ).
[^6623]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 4, Glory (Acrostic: σ - Помы́сливъ).
[^6624]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 4, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6625]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 5 Heirmos. Allusion to Isaiah 26:9.
[^6626]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 5, Troparion 1 (Acrostic: φ - Помышлѧ́юще).
[^6627]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 5, Troparion 2 (Acrostic: ε - Ѻ҆́тче).
[^6628]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 5, Glory (Acrostic: ρ - Оу҆правлѧ́й).
[^6629]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 5, Both now... (Theotokion). Allusion to Hebrews 1:3 ('upholding all things by the word of His power').
[^6630]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 6 Heirmos. Typology of Jonah 2:2–7.
[^6631]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 6, Troparion 1 (completed from p635; chanted twice per rubric).
[^6632]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 6, Glory.
[^6633]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 6, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6634]: Rubric following Ode 6 of the Sunday Midnight Office Canon.
[^6635]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Sessional Hymn after Ode 6. Podoben: 'Fulfilling the dispensation for our sake' (Є҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ). Allusion to Isaiah 6:2–3 and Romans 8:31.
[^6636]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Sessional Theotokion after Ode 6.
[^6637]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 7 Heirmos. Typology of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:49–52).
[^6638]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 7, Troparion 1.
[^6639]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 7, Troparion 2.
[^6640]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 7, Glory.
[^6641]: Conclusion of the Theotokion of Ode 7 from p636.
[^6642]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 8 Heirmos. Typology of Daniel 3:49–50 and 1 Kings / 3 Kingdoms 18:38.
[^6643]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 8, Troparion 1.
[^6644]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 8, Troparion 2 (Slavonic 'Холи́тель' = one who cherishes/favors, i.e. Lover of mercy).
[^6645]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 8, Glory. Patristic Light-theology (Light from Light).
[^6646]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 8, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6647]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 9 Heirmos. Allusion to 1 Timothy 6:16 and Luke 1:48.
[^6648]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Allusion to Isaiah 6:2.
[^6649]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 9, Troparion 2.
[^6650]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 9, Glory.
[^6651]: Tone 6 Sunday Midnight Office, Canon to the Most Holy Trinity, Ode 9, Both now... (Theotokion).
[^6652]: Rubric indicating the Troparia to the Holy Trinity by Saint Gregory the Sinaite (c. 1260–1346) and the conclusion of the Sunday Midnight Office.
[^6653]: Rubric opening Sunday Matins (Въ Недѣ́лю на Оу҆́тренни).
[^6654]: Rubrics for the opening of Sunday Matins: Six Psalms, 'God is the Lord' (Psalm 117:27), Resurrection Troparion ('The angelic powers were upon Thy tomb' / 'А҆́гг҃лскїа си́лы на гро́бѣ твое́мъ'), Troparion of the Saint from the Menaion, Theotokion, and Kathismata from the Psalter.
[^6655]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 1 after the 1st Kathisma. Allusion to Matthew 20:1–16 and John 20:1–2.
[^6656]: Psalm 9:33 (LXX 9:33; Hebrew/KJV 10:12).
[^6657]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 2 after the 1st Kathisma. Allusion to Matthew 27:66.
[^6658]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Sessional Theotokion after the 1st Kathisma.
[^6659]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 1 after the 2nd Kathisma. Allusion to John 20:15 and Matthew 28:4.
[^6660]: Psalm 9:2 (LXX 9:2; Hebrew/KJV 9:1).
[^6661]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 2 after the 2nd Kathisma. Typology of the sign of Jonah (Jonah 2:1–10; Matthew 12:40) and the prophecy of Righteous Symeon the God-receiver (Luke 2:34).
[^6662]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Sessional Theotokion after the 2nd Kathisma (commencing on p638). Typology of Gideon's fleece (Judges 6:36–40) and David's Psalm 71:6 (LXX 71:6).
[^6663]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hypakoe (Ѵ҆пакоѝ) following the Evlogetaria / Amomos (Psalm 118).
[^6664]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent (Степе́нна), Antiphon 1, Stanza 1. Allusion to Psalm 122:1 (LXX 122:1; 'Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes').
[^6665]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 1, Stanza 2. Allusion to Psalm 122:3 (LXX 122:3; 'Have mercy upon us, O Lord, have mercy upon us').
[^6666]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 1, Glory (Pneumatology of the Holy Spirit as divine Breath and Lifegiver).
[^6667]: Rubric indicating repetition of the Glory sticheron for Both now... in the Hymns of Ascent.
[^6668]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 2, Stanza 1. Allusion to Psalm 123:1–2 (LXX 123:1–2; 'If it had not been that the Lord was with us').
[^6669]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 2, Stanza 2. Allusion to Psalm 123:6–7 (LXX 123:6–7; 'our soul is escaped as a bird out of the snare of the fowlers').
[^6670]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 2, Glory (Pneumatology of the Holy Spirit and theosis / ѡ҆боже́нїе).
[^6671]: Rubric indicating repetition of the Glory sticheron for Both now... in Antiphon 2.
[^6672]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 3, Stanza 1. Allusion to Psalm 124:1 (LXX 124:1; 'They that trust in the Lord shall be as Mount Sion').
[^6673]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 3, Stanza 2. Allusion to Psalm 124:3 (LXX 124:3; 'that the righteous put not forth their hands unto iniquity').
[^6674]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Hymns of Ascent, Antiphon 3, Glory (Sovereign cosmic dominion of the Holy Spirit).
[^6675]: Rubric indicating repetition of the Glory sticheron for Both now... in Antiphon 3.
[^6676]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Prokeimenon. Psalm 79:3 (LXX 79:3; Hebrew/KJV 80:2).
[^6677]: Psalm 79:2 (LXX 79:2; Hebrew/KJV 80:1).
[^6678]: Matins Gospel versicle. Psalm 150:6 (LXX 150:6).
[^6679]: Psalm 150:1 (LXX 150:1).
[^6680]: Rubrics for the Sunday Matins Resurrection Gospel, the hymn 'Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ' (Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во ви́дѣвше), Psalm 50 (LXX 50), and the accompanying liturgical order.
[^6681]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon (composed by Saint John of Damascus), Ode 1 Heirmos. Typology of the Red Sea crossing (Exodus 14:21–31).
[^6682]: Refrain to the holy Resurrection (Сла́ва Гдⷭ҇и ст҃о́мꙋ воскресе́нїю твоемꙋ̀).
[^6683]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1.
[^6684]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2.
[^6685]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion.
[^6686]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 1 Heirmos. Typology of the Red Sea crossing (Exodus 14:28) and the burial of Christ. Heirmos ascribed to the nun Cassia / Saint Cosmas.
[^6687]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1.
[^6688]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2. Allusion to Psalm 87:7 (LXX 87:7; 'They laid Me in the lowest pit').
[^6689]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion. Typological Marian titles (tabernacle, candlestick, temple, altar-table, holy mountain).
[^6690]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Acrostic (Greek: Μῆτερ Θεοῦ, ἄφθονον δός μοι χάριν; Slavonic: Ма́ти Бж҃їа, незави́стнꙋю мнѣ̀ да́ждь благода́ть).
[^6691]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1 Heirmos incipit (chanted to the melody of the first Heirmos: 'Having walked on foot upon the deep').
[^6692]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1. Contrast between Eve's disobedience in Eden and the Theotokos bearing Christ (Genesis 3:6; 1 Corinthians 15:20).
[^6693]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2 (text concludes on p641; Patristic metaphor of Christ the divine Pearl conceived from heavenly lightning).
[^6694]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, conclusion of Troparion 2 (continued from p640). Allusion to Christ as the divine Pearl (би́серъ) born of the Virgin.
[^6695]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 3. Messianic prophecy of the Patriarch Jacob concerning Shiloh, the 'Expectation of the nations' (Genesis 49:10).
[^6696]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 4. Fulfillment of Genesis 49:10 regarding the scepter departing from Judah.
[^6697]: Matins Katavasia incipit: 'I shall open my mouth' (Ѿве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моѧ̑; Ode 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos by Saint John of Damascus).
[^6698]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 3 Heirmos. Typology of Hannah's prayer (1 Kings / 1 Samuel 2:1–2).
[^6699]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1. Cosmic quaking at the Crucifixion held fast by the Creator's hand (Matthew 27:51).
[^6700]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2. The overthrow of personified death by the resurrection of Christ (1 Corinthians 15:54–55).
[^6701]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 3, Theotokion. The mystery of the Virgin's virginitas post partum.
[^6702]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 3 Heirmos. Christ who founded the earth upon the waters (Psalm 135:6 / LXX 135:6) hanging upon Golgotha.
[^6703]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1. The third-day Resurrection and the greeting of the Myrrh-bearers (Matthew 28:9).
[^6704]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2. Deliverance of mankind from hades and death.
[^6705]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 3, Theotokion. The Incarnation delivering the fallen Adam from demonic deception.
[^6706]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3 Heirmos incipit.
[^6707]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, conclusion of Troparion 1 (continued from p641). Deification and eternal incorruption of human nature assumed by Christ.
[^6708]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2. Angelic wonder at the Incarnation of God from the Virgin.
[^6709]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 3. Daniel's vision of the stone cut without hands from the mountain shattering the idol (Daniel 2:34–35, 45).
[^6710]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 4. Inadequacy of human language to extol the Virgin's seedless conception.
[^6711]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 4 Heirmos. Typological heirmos of Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3:2; 1 Corinthians 1:24).
[^6712]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1. The Cross as the Tree of Life watered by blood and water from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34).
[^6713]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2. Overthrow of the serpent's deceptive promise of false deification in Eden (Genesis 3:5).
[^6714]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 4, Theotokion. Truly unsearchable mystery of the Theotokos' childbirth.
[^6715]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 4 Heirmos. Prophecy of Habakkuk regarding divine kenosis (Habakkuk 3:2–3; Philippians 2:7).
[^6716]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1. Annulment of the primal curse through the bodily Crucifixion (Galatians 3:13).
[^6717]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2. Incorruption of Christ's flesh in the tomb (Psalm 15:10 / LXX 15:10; Acts 2:27, 31).
[^6718]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 4, Theotokion. Commissioning of the Myrrh-bearing women as Apostles to the Apostles (Matthew 28:9–10).
[^6719]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4 Heirmos incipit.
[^6720]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 1. Blessing the Ever-Virgin Mother of God (Luke 1:48).
[^6721]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 2. Birth of Christ as the unwaning Light in worldly darkness (John 1:5, 9).
[^6722]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 3. Flowering of grace and cessation of the Old Testament Law (John 1:17).
[^6723]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 4. Reversal of the fall through Christ the Tree of Life (Genesis 3:6; Revelation 22:2).
[^6724]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 5 Heirmos. Allusion to Isaiah's vigil from night unto dawn (Isaiah 26:9).
[^6725]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Removal of the Cherubim's flaming sword for the Penitent Thief (Genesis 3:24; Luke 23:43).
[^6726]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 2. Deliverance from return to dust through Christ's Resurrection (Genesis 3:19; 1 Corinthians 15:53).
[^6727]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 5, Theotokion. Invocation of the Theotokos as invincible protection.
[^6728]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 5 Heirmos. Prophecy of bodily resurrection in Isaiah 26:19.
[^6729]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Voluntary crucifixion to heal Adam's sinful eating (Genesis 3:6).
[^6730]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 5, conclusion of Troparion 2 (continued from p643). The hallowing of fallen human nature and enlightenment of the world.
[^6731]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 5, Theotokion. Incorrupt virginity of the Mother of God and intercession for her flock.
[^6732]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5 Heirmos incipit.
[^6733]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1. The Virgin as the divine abode of the infant Word.
[^6734]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2. Spiritual beauty of the Bride of God sealed with virginity.
[^6735]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 3. Rebuking deniers of the Theotokos and extolling her as gate of divine light (Ezekiel 44:2).
[^6736]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 6 Heirmos. Typology of Jonah in the deep (Jonah 2:2–9).
[^6737]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Blotting out the handwritten debt through the piercing of Christ's side (Colossians 2:14; John 19:34).
[^6738]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2. The Good Shepherd seeking the lost sheep and bearing it upon His shoulders (Luke 15:4–5).
[^6739]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 6, Theotokion. Supplication to the Theotokos to still passions through the divine Helmsman.
[^6740]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 6 Heirmos. Jonah's emergence from the sea beast and warning against vain vanities (Jonah 2:8 / LXX 2:9).
[^6741]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 6, conclusion of Troparion 1 (continued from p644). Impassible divinity enduring suffering in human flesh between two thieves (Luke 23:33).
[^6742]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2. The Lord's voluntary burial and joyful greeting to the Myrrh-bearers (Matthew 28:9).
[^6743]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 6, Theotokion. Marian supplication for divine mercy.
[^6744]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6 Heirmos incipit.
[^6745]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Mosaic symbols of the Virgin: ark of the covenant, table of showbread, golden lampstand, and jar of manna (Hebrews 9:2–4).
[^6746]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 2. Overthrow of death through the Fruit of Mary's womb.
[^6747]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 3. Transition from shadowy Mosaic figures to Gospel grace.
[^6748]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Kontakion. Attributed to Saint Romanos the Melodist.
[^6749]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Ikos, conclusion (continued from p645). Universal victory of Christ over hades and the serpent.
[^6750]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 7 Heirmos. Typology of the three holy youths in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:24–50).
[^6751]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 1. Cosmic darkness during the Crucifixion (Matthew 27:45).
[^6752]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 2. Illumination of hades and the joyful dancing of Adam.
[^6753]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 7, Triadikon. Doxology to the Holy Trinity.
[^6754]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 7 Heirmos. Typology of the furnace and Christ's rest in the tomb.
[^6755]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 1. Shattering of the brazen gates of hades (Psalm 106:16 / LXX 106:16).
[^6756]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 2. Christ granting resurrection unto all who confess Him orthodoxly.
[^6757]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 7, Theotokion. One of the Trinity incarnate in two distinct natures.
[^6758]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7 Heirmos incipit.
[^6759]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 1. The unconsuming furnace fire prefiguring the virginal conception of the divine Fire.
[^6760]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, conclusion of Troparion 2 (continued from p646). Fulfillment of the Magnificat prophecy (Luke 1:48).
[^6761]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 3. Christ crushing the destructive fangs of the infernal serpent (Genesis 3:15).
[^6762]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 4. Mary as the living propitiatory throne carrying God Who rides upon the Cherubim (Psalm 17:11 / LXX 17:11).
[^6763]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 8 Heirmos. Elijah consuming the water-soaked sacrifice with fire (3 Kings / 1 Kings 18:38) and the three youths in the furnace (Daniel 3:49–50).
[^6764]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1. Indictment of the crucifixion through envy (Matthew 27:18).
[^6765]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2. Christ lifting the fallen man from the dunghill of mortality (1 Kings / 1 Samuel 2:8; Psalm 112:7 / LXX 112:7).
[^6766]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 8, Triadikon. Doxology to the consubstantial Trinity.
[^6767]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 8 Heirmos. Heavens and earth shaken by the burial of the Living God.
[^6768]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1. Awe of creation at the voluntary descent and victory of the Most High.
[^6769]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2. Thanksgiving for liberation from the dungeon of hades.
[^6770]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 8, conclusion of Theotokion (continued from p647). Allusion to the Song of the Three Children (Daniel 3:57).
[^6771]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8 Heirmos incipit.
[^6772]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 1. Psalm 44:10 (LXX 44:10; 'at Thy right hand did stand the queen in vesture of inwrought gold').
[^6773]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 2. The Creator assuming human flesh from the Virgin to renew creation on high.
[^6774]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 3. The Virgin as the pure dwelling-place of the Incarnate Word.
[^6775]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 4. Typology of the golden lampstand (Exodus 25:31–40).
[^6776]: Rubric for the Magnificat (Luke 1:46–55; Честнѣ́йшꙋю / 'More honorable than the Cherubim').
[^6777]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 9 Heirmos. Contrast between the unapproachable divine essence and the visible Incarnation through Mary (1 Timothy 6:16).
[^6778]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Christ's voluntary suffering destroying suffering and granting impassibility.
[^6779]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2. Incorruption of Christ's body and harrowing of hades (Psalm 15:10 / LXX 15:10).
[^6780]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, Triadikon. Doxology to the Trinity.
[^6781]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 9 Heirmos. The famous Holy Saturday Heirmos 'Lament Me not, O Mother' (Не рыда́й менѐ Ма́ти).
[^6782]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, conclusion of Troparion 1 (continued from p648). Overthrow of hades and bestowal of resurrection.
[^6783]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2. Creation robed in white joy at the angelic proclamation to the Myrrh-bearers (Matthew 28:5–7).
[^6784]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 3. Defeat of death and hades (Hosea 13:14; 1 Corinthians 15:55).
[^6785]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Cross and Resurrection Canon, Ode 9, Theotokion. Bethlehem opening the gates of Eden through the birth of Christ.
[^6786]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9 Heirmos incipit.
[^6787]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Balaam's prophecy of the Star out of Jacob (Numbers 24:17).
[^6788]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 2. Devotional dedication of the hymn to the Theotokos.
[^6789]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 3. The Theotokos as the spiritual loom weaving the fleshly garment of the divine Word.
[^6790]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 4. Abolition of death's somber dominion through Christ's birth.
[^6791]: Rubrics for Little Litany, the hymn 'Holy is the Lord our God' (Свѧ́тъ Гдⷭ҇ь Бг҃ъ на́шъ; Psalm 98:9 / LXX 98:9), and the appointed Sunday Morning Exapostilarion (Е҆ѯапостїла́рїй оу҆́тренній).
[^6792]: Opening rubric for the Stichera at the Praises (Хвали́тныѧ стїхи̑ры) at Sunday Matins, Tone 6.
[^6793]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises (Lauds), Sticheron 1 (Resurrectional).
[^6794]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 2 (Resurrectional).
[^6795]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 3 (Resurrectional).
[^6796]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 4 (Resurrectional). Allusion to 1 Corinthians 15:4 ('according to the Scriptures').
[^6797]: Rubric introducing the Anatolian (Eastern) Stichera (А҆нато́лїєвы стїхи̑ры) in Tone 6.
[^6798]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 5 (Anatolian 1). Christ coming forth from the tomb as a bridegroom from his chamber (Psalm 18:6 / LXX 18:6).
[^6799]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 6 (Anatolian 2). Rebuking the bribing of the Roman guards by the chief priests (Matthew 28:11–15).
[^6800]: Psalm 9:33 (LXX 9:33; Hebrew/KJV 10:12).
[^6801]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 7 (Anatolian 3). Christ emerging from the sealed tomb as He passed through closed doors (John 20:19).
[^6802]: Psalm 9:2 (LXX 9:2; Hebrew/KJV 9:1).
[^6803]: Tone 6 Sunday Matins, Stichera at the Praises, Sticheron 8 (Anatolian 4; text concludes on p651). The angel seated upon the stone announcing the Resurrection to the Myrrh-bearers (Matthew 28:1–7; Luke 24:1–8).
[^6804]: Rubrics concluding Tone 6 Sunday Matins: the 6th Eothinon Gospel Sticheron (Сла́ва: Стїхи́ра оу҆́треннаѧ є҆ѵ́лскаѧ), the Sunday Theotokion (*Преблагослове́нна є҆сѝ, Бцⷣе Дѣ́во*), the Great Doxology, the even-tone Sunday Troparion (*Воскре́съ и҆зъ гро́ба*), the litanies, and the dismissal.
[^6805]: The Beatitudes (Блаже́нна) in Tone 6 for the Sunday Divine Liturgy, chanted with the Beatitude verses (Matthew 5:3–12).
[^6806]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Refrain: Luke 23:42 ('Lord, remember me when Thou comest into Thy kingdom').
[^6807]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 1: The typological contrast between the tree of the Fall (Genesis 3) and the Tree of the Cross, through which salvation was granted unto the penitent thief.
[^6808]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 2: Confession of Christ's voluntary Crucifixion, Burial, and third-day Resurrection.
[^6809]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 3: Christ shattering the gates of bronze and bars of iron of hades (Psalm 106:16 / LXX 106:16).
[^6810]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 4: Christ's Resurrection fulfilling His prophecy before the Passion (Matthew 16:21).
[^6811]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 5: The Apostles as the light of the world (Matthew 5:14).
[^6812]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Triadikon: Doxology to the Holy Trinity.
[^6813]: Tone 6 Sunday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary as the gate opened unto God (Ezekiel 44:2).
[^6814]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening, opening Vespers of Monday dedicated to the Holy Bodiless Powers.
[^6815]: Opening rubric for the Penitential Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried' (Psalm 140 / LXX 140) at Sunday Evening Vespers.
[^6816]: Melodic model: *Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃лскїѧ си́лы)*. The acrostic attributes this sequence of penitential stichera to Saint Joseph the Hymnographer.
[^6817]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 1 (text concludes from p651): Invoking the intercession of the Mother of God and the Angelic choirs.
[^6818]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 2: Exhortation to the soul to repent, with allusion to the barren fig tree (Matthew 21:19; Luke 13:6–9).
[^6819]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 3: Allusion to the Last Judgment and the repentance of the Prodigal Son (Luke 15:11–32).
[^6820]: Rubric directing the insertion of the stichera from the Menaion for the saint of the day, or alternatively these three stichera to the Holy Bodiless Powers to the melodic model *Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́внѡ воскре́слъ є҆сѝ)*.
[^6821]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 4 (Bodiless Powers 1): The Angelic Powers interceding before the heavenly throne for human peace.
[^6822]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 5 (Bodiless Powers 2): The nine ranks of Angels choir round about the throne of the King of all.
[^6823]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 6 (Bodiless Powers 3): The Holy Trinity creating the incorporeal and immaterial angelic intelligences.
[^6824]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Theotokion: The Virgin Mary exalted above the Cherubim, Seraphim, and Thrones through the Annunciation of the Archangel (Luke 1:26–38).
[^6825]: Rubrics for the evening entrance: *Phos Hilaron* (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*), the Sunday evening Prokeimenon, and the evening prayer *Сподо́би Гдⷭ҇и*.
[^6826]: Rubric for the Penitential Stichera at the Aposticha (Стїхи̑ры на стїхо́внѣ) for Sunday Evening Vespers in Tone 6.
[^6827]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 1: Allusion to Matthew 25:12 ('Truly I say unto you, I know you not').
[^6828]: Psalm 122:1–2 (LXX 122:1–2).
[^6829]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 2: Prayer for heartfelt compunction and tears before the end.
[^6830]: Psalm 122:3–4 (LXX 122:3–4).
[^6831]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon): Commemoration of the holy martyrs who remained steadfast under persecution.
[^6832]: Tone 6 Sunday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Theotokion: Marian typology of the sealed door (Ezekiel 44:2) and heavenly bridal chamber.
[^6833]: Concluding rubrics of Sunday Evening Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion Prayers, Troparion, Augmented Litany, and Dismissal.
[^6834]: Title and rubric for the Sunday Compline Canon (Канѡ́нъ на Повече́рїи) to the Most Holy Theotokos in Tone 6.
[^6835]: Refrain to the Theotokos at Compline.
[^6836]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Christ as the Radiance (*сїѧ́нїе*) of the Father (Hebrews 1:3).
[^6837]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2: Joining the greeting of Archangel Gabriel at the Annunciation (Luke 1:28).
[^6838]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 3: Christ begotten impassibly of the Father before all ages and born in two natures (divine and human) from the Virgin.
[^6839]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 4: Affirmation of the perpetual virginity (*semper virgo*) of the Theotokos before, during, and after childbirth.
[^6840]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3 Heirmos: Canticle of Hannah (1 Kingdoms 2:1–10 / LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:1–10).
[^6841]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 1: The blameless holiness of the Theotokos as Mother of the Creator.
[^6842]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2 (concluding from p653): Cry for deliverance from the storm of passions and spiritual descent into the abyss.
[^6843]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 3: Christ as the unapproachable Sun of Life.
[^6844]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 4: Purification through the fear of God and vesting in the radiant garment of virtues.
[^6845]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2–19 (Canticle of Habakkuk).
[^6846]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 1: Christ as the Sun of Righteousness (Malachi 4:2).
[^6847]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 2: Petition that the supplicant's infirmity receive effective healing through the Theotokos.
[^6848]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 3: Bestow thy rich compassions upon those who enter the temple.
[^6849]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 4: The divine Logos delivering the world from the irrationality of passions (*безслове́сїе страсте́й*).
[^6850]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9–20 (Canticle of Isaiah).
[^6851]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1: Awakening the soul from the sleep of sin through the divine ray of the Incarnate Christ.
[^6852]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2: Parable of the Good Samaritan pouring oil and wine upon the wounds of the traveler fallen among thieves (Luke 10:30–37).
[^6853]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 3: Invoking the Theotokos for the healing of both bodily and spiritual diseases.
