[^6854]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 4 (concluding from p654): The Holy Spirit overshadowing the Virgin, making her the temple of the Holy Trinity (Luke 1:35).
[^6855]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:2–10 (Canticle of Jonah).
[^6856]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 1: Dispersing the darkness of the soul through the light born of the Virgin.
[^6857]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 2: Seeking refuge under the mighty shelter of the Virgin against visible and invisible enemies.
[^6858]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 3: The Incarnation of the Creator restoring fallen human nature derived from Adam.
[^6859]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 4: The Virgin praised by the angelic hosts; petition for tranquility against demonic delusions.
[^6860]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Sessional Hymn after Ode 6: Prayer for the defense of the Virgin's inheritance against the assaults of enemies.
[^6861]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:26–56 (Canticle of the Three Holy Children in the Furnace).
[^6862]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 1: Enlightenment from the darkness of ignorance unto the true worship of the God of our fathers.
[^6863]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 2: The Virgin as the receptacle of the pre-eternal Light Who shone forth impassibly from the Father.
[^6864]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 3: Petition for the Mother of God to make Christ propitious toward those who trust in her.
[^6865]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 4 (concluding from p655): Petition for cleansing tears of repentance to wash away the shame of sin.
[^6866]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3:57–88 (The Song of the Three Holy Children). The heirmos unites the miracle of the furnace with the sacrifice of Elijah consumed by fire upon Mount Carmel despite being drenched with water (3 Kingdoms 18:33–38 / 1 Kings 18:33–38).
[^6867]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 1: Snatching the soul from the violence of passions into the love of the Creator.
[^6868]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Prayer for raising from the bed of sickness and granting bodily and spiritual health.
[^6869]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 3: Quenching the fiery flame of passions like the three holy youths delivered from the furnace.
[^6870]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 4: Bold confidence against the howling adversary through hope in the Mother of God.
[^6871]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9 Heirmos: Luke 1:46–55 (The Magnificat) and Luke 1:68–79 (Benedictus). The incomprehensible God made visible through the Incarnation of the Word.
[^6872]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 1: Deliverance from tribulations and restoration of health to glorify the Virgin.
[^6873]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 2: The curse of Eve abolished by the childbirth of the Virgin, who bore the Fountain of blessing.
[^6874]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 3: The womb of the Virgin as the vessel of the eternal Light of the Father.
[^6875]: Tone 6 Sunday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 4 (concluding from p656): Confession of the unwedded Mother of God who bore the timeless Son in time.
[^6876]: Concluding rubrics of Sunday Compline: *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), Trisagion Prayers, Troparion, and Dismissal.
[^6877]: Rubric for the Penitential Sessional Hymns after the 1st Kathisma at Monday Matins in Tone 6.
[^6878]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 1 after Kathisma 1: Contemplation of the Day of Judgment; invocation of the Holy Trinity (Father, Son, and Holy Spirit).
[^6879]: Psalm 6:2 (LXX 6:2).
[^6880]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 2 after Kathisma 1: Allusion to the valley of weeping (Psalm 83:7 / LXX 83:7) and the public exposure of sins at the Last Judgment.
[^6881]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 1, Theotokion: Praise of the Mother of God as holier than the holy Angels.
[^6882]: Rubric for the Sessional Hymns after the 2nd Kathisma at Monday Matins in Tone 6.
[^6883]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 1 after Kathisma 2: Penitential prayer of sinners having no defense before the Master.
[^6884]: Psalm 37:2 (LXX 37:2).
[^6885]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 2 after Kathisma 2: Allusion to Psalm 78:8–9 (LXX 78:8–9) and Isaiah 64:8–9 ('We are all the work of Thy hand').
[^6886]: Psalm 67:36 (LXX 67:36).
[^6887]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 3 (Martyrikon) after Kathisma 2: Light dawning for the righteous (Psalm 96:11 / LXX 96:11); the martyrs shining as heavenly luminaries.
[^6888]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 2, Theotokion (concluding from p657): The ancient liturgical chant *Въ хра́мѣ стоя́ще сла́вы твоеѧ̀, на небесѝ стоя́ти мни́мъ* ('Standing in the temple of thy glory, we seem to stand in heaven').
[^6889]: Rubric for the Sessional Hymns after the 3rd Kathisma at Monday Matins in Tone 6, sung to the melodic model *Tone 6, Podoben: "The Hope" (Оу҆пова́нїе)*.
[^6890]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 1 after Kathisma 3: Fear before the judgment seat of Christ; invoking the intercession of the Angels.
[^6891]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn 2 after Kathisma 3: Rebuking the soul for spiritual slothfulness; plea for mercy in the hour of condemnation.
[^6892]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 3, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary addressed as Hope and Haven of the faithful, praying together with the Bodiless Powers.
[^6893]: Title and acrostic rubric for the first Canon of Monday Matins in Tone 6, dedicated to repentance and the Holy Martyrs. Composed by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the acrostic: *Ѻ҆́чїю мое́ю Хрⷭ҇тѐ, пла́чь прїимѝ* ('Accept, O Christ, the weeping of mine eyes').
[^6894]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1 Heirmos: Moses' song of victory over Pharaoh (Exodus 15:1–19 / Ode 1 of Moses).
[^6895]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1 (concluding from p658): Parable of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30–35), seeking spiritual healing from Christ.
[^6896]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2: Contrast between the soul becoming a den of thieves (Matthew 21:13) and Christ born in a cave/manger (Luke 2:7).
[^6897]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs casting down the demonic adversary and receiving crowns woven by God.
[^6898]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs drowning the noetic Pharaoh in their blood and crossing over into the heavenly Promised Land.
[^6899]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 5 (Theotokion): Invoking the Mother of God together with the Martyrs, Prophets, and Angels.
[^6900]: Title and acrostic rubric for the second Canon of Monday Matins in Tone 6, dedicated to the Holy Heavenly Bodiless Angels. Composed by Saint Theophanes the Branded (Graptos), bearing the acrostic: *Шесто́е быва́етъ оу҆́мныхъ пѣ́нїе* ('The sixth singing of the noetic hosts').
[^6901]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 1 Heirmos (concluding from p658): Song of victory upon the crossing of the Red Sea (Exodus 14–15).
[^6902]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Praying to be enlightened by the lightning-like radiance of the heavenly ranks.
[^6903]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 1, Troparion 2: The Triune God creating and adorning the angelic hosts by the Word and the Holy Spirit.
[^6904]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 1, Troparion 3 (Theotokion): The Theotokos exalted as Lady of creation by bearing the incarnate Creator.
[^6905]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3 Heirmos: Establishing the soul upon the rock of faith (1 Kingdoms 2:1–10 / LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:1–10).
[^6906]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1: Confessing the struggle between promises of repentance and recurrence of sins.
[^6907]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2: Warning against presumption upon God's longsuffering and the peril of sudden death.
[^6908]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The holy martyrs embracing torments and trials as spiritual food for the sake of eternal life.
[^6909]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs steadfast upon the rock of faith, unmoved by manifold tortures.
[^6910]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 5 (Theotokion): The Virgin Mary as the new Paradise bearing the Tree of Life (Christ), reversing Adam's condemnation.
[^6911]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 3 Heirmos: Establishing the faithful upon the rock of divine confession.
[^6912]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 3, Troparion 1: The Divine Nature creating all rational beings out of nothing, hymned with the Thrice-Holy hymn (Isaiah 6:3) in three hypostases.
[^6913]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 3, Troparion 2: God pouring forth His goodness to establish the bodiless angelic choirs in communion with Himself.
[^6914]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 3, Troparion 3 (Theotokion): The Lawgiver incarnate of the Virgin suffering for humanity and granting remission of sins.
[^6915]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:3 (Canticle of Habakkuk).
[^6916]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1: Supplication for customary divine mercy and remission of many offenses.
[^6917]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2: Christ as the Way, the Truth, and the Life (John 14:6), guarding the soul from death-bearing paths.
[^6918]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs quenching material fire through their burning wounds and the dew of the Holy Spirit.
[^6919]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs drinking of the torrent of divine pleasure (Psalm 35:9 / LXX 35:9).
[^6920]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 5 (Theotokion): Recourse to the protection of the Theotokos after a life spent in spiritual slothfulness.
[^6921]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2–19 (Canticle of Habakkuk).
[^6922]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 4, Troparion 1: The bodiless angelic intelligences standing incorruptibly around the heavenly throne.
[^6923]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 4, Troparion 2: The angelic assemblies sanctified by the Holy Spirit and deified by perpetual contemplation of the Primal Good.
[^6924]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 4, Troparion 3 (Theotokion): The primal curse of Eve abolished by the Theotokos giving birth to the ever-flowing Fountain of blessing.
[^6925]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9 (Canticle of Isaiah).
[^6926]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1: Christ as the Rod from the root of Jesse (Isaiah 11:1), withering passions and planting holy fear.
[^6927]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 2: Following the contrition of the Publican (Luke 18:13) to escape everlasting lamentation.
[^6928]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The holy martyrs enduring bodily wounds with joy through their longing for Christ.
[^6929]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs crying aloud in bodily suffering and being heard by Christ.
[^6930]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Theotokion: The Mother of God as the heavenly portal through which repentant sinners enter into pardon (Ezekiel 44:2).
[^6931]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9 (Canticle of Isaiah).
[^6932]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 5, Troparion 1: The prophetic vision of Isaiah (Isaiah 6:1–3) beholding the Lord upon the Cherubic throne.
[^6933]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 5, Troparion 2: The vision of the Prophet Daniel (Daniel 7:9–10) beholding the Ancient of Days attended by thousand thousands and ten thousand times ten thousand of Angels.
[^6934]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 5, Theotokion: Christ beautiful beyond the sons of men (Psalm 44:3 / LXX 44:3) taking human nature from the Virgin.
[^6935]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:2–10 (Canticle of Jonah).
[^6936]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 1: Christ Who gave sight to the blind man (John 9) opening the noetic eyes of the soul.
[^6937]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2: Prayer for tears of compunction and the lifegiving waters of repentance.
[^6938]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The spiritual valleys of martyrdom blossoming with lilies of healing (Song of Songs 2:1).
[^6939]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs loosed from corruptible flesh and bound by divine love to the Master.
[^6940]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Theotokion: Supplication unto the Theotokos for purification of a defiled heart.
[^6941]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 6 Heirmos: Fleeing from the tempest-tossed sea of life unto the tranquil haven of divine mercy (Jonah 2:2–3).
[^6942]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 6, Troparion 1: Jacob's ladder (Genesis 28:12) with Angels ascending and descending, prefiguring the Incarnation.
[^6943]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 6, Troparion 2: Jacob (Israel) rejoicing at the angelic host encamped around him (Genesis 32:1–2).
[^6944]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 6, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary as the spiritual mountain (Daniel 2:34–35) higher than the angelic hosts.
[^6945]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:26–56 (Canticle of the Three Holy Children in the Furnace).
[^6946]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 1: Confession of spiritual blindness caused by intense sinfulness.
[^6947]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 2: Hope in divine loving-kindness and supplication for remission of debts.
[^6948]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): Invoking the intercession of the holy martyrs clothed in heavenly glory.
[^6949]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs rejecting unrighteous imperial decrees and striving lawfully according to the faith (2 Timothy 2:5).
[^6950]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Theotokion: The Master taking the form of a servant (Philippians 2:7) to liberate mankind from the servitude of passions.
[^6951]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:49–50, the Angel descending into the furnace and making the flame like a moist whistling wind.
[^6952]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 7, Troparion 1: The holy Angels shining in beauty and singing unceasing doxology unto Christ.
[^6953]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 7, Troparion 2: The angelic intelligences illuminated by the unapproachable light of the Godhead.
[^6954]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 7, Theotokion (concluding from p662): The Archangel's salutation (Luke 1:28) and the renewal of corrupt human nature through the Virgin's womb.
[^6955]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3:57–88 (The Song of the Three Holy Children).
[^6956]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1: Allusion to the parable of the rich man and Lazarus (Luke 16:19–31) and the unquenchable fire (Mark 9:43).
[^6957]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Allusion to Matthew 24:20 ('Pray ye that your flight be not in the winter, neither on the sabbath day').
[^6958]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs conquering deceit and enduring torments while praising Christ.
[^6959]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The flame of persecution quenched by the dew of patience and divine love.
[^6960]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary more honorable than the Cherubim, sanctified to become the Mother of God.
[^6961]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3 (the furnace of Babylon) and 3 Kingdoms 18:38 (LXX 3 Kingdoms 18:38 / 1 Kings 18:38, the sacrifice of Elijah consumed by divine fire).
[^6962]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 8, Troparion 1: The Prophet Elisha guarded by invisible chariots and horsemen of fire (4 Kingdoms 6:15–17 / LXX 4 Kingdoms 6:15–17).
[^6963]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Supplication to the holy Archistrategoi standing before the dread throne of God.
[^6964]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 8, Theotokion: Archangel Gabriel's greeting to the Theotokos (Luke 1:28) who bore the incarnate Word.
[^6965]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9 Heirmos: Luke 1:28, the greeting of Gabriel and the birth of the Creator from the Virgin.
[^6966]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 1: Joseph the All-Comely distributing grain in Egypt (Genesis 41:55–57) contrasted with the famine of virtues in the prodigal soul (Amos 8:11).
[^6967]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2: The threefold models of repentance: Peter weeping bitterly (Matthew 26:75), the publican sighing (Luke 18:13), and the prodigal crying from the depths (Luke 15:18–19).
[^6968]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The victorious martyrs crowned from on high by the power of Christ.
[^6969]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The sanctifying feast of the holy martyrs celebrated in Orthodox faith.
[^6970]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Theotokion: The Mother of God bearing the Light of impassibility.
[^6971]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 9 Heirmos: Luke 1:46–55 (The Magnificat); the invisible God revealed in the flesh through the Virgin Mary.
[^6972]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 9, Troparion 1: 2 Samuel 24:15–17 (LXX 2 Kingdoms 24:15–17) and 1 Chronicles 21:14–27, the Angel sheathing his sword at the threshing floor of Ornan/Araunah after David's prayer.
[^6973]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 9, Troparion 2: The angelic ministers standing around the King of all, interceding for the healing of afflicted mankind.
[^6974]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Canon to the Bodiless Powers, Ode 9, Theotokion: The nine ranks of celestial hierarchies (Principalities, Archangels, Dominions, Seraphim, Powers, Authorities, Angels, Thrones, Cherubim) glorifying the divine maternity of the Virgin.
[^6975]: Rubrics concluding Monday Matins: *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the Augmented Litany, the Exapostilarion, and the dismissal psalms.
[^6976]: Rubric for the Penitential Stichera at the Aposticha (Стїхи̑ры на стїхо́внѣ) for Monday Matins in Tone 6.
[^6977]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 1: The parable of the traveler wounded by thieves (Luke 10:30), crying to Christ the Physician.
[^6978]: Psalm 89:14, 16 (LXX 89:14, 16).
[^6979]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 2: Christ as Physician of souls and bodies, granting tears of compunction and remission of debts.
[^6980]: Psalm 89:17 (LXX 89:17).
[^6981]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon): The commemoration of the Martyrs uniting heaven and earth in festive joy.
[^6982]: Tone 6 Monday Matins, Aposticha, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary receiving the salutation of Archangel Gabriel and carrying the Savior upon her arms.
[^6983]: Concluding rubrics of Monday Matins: *It is good to give praise* (Psalm 91:2 / LXX 91:2), Trisagion Prayers, Troparion, Litany, and Dismissal.
[^6984]: The Beatitudes (Блаже́нна) in Tone 6 for the Monday Divine Liturgy.
[^6985]: Tone 6 Monday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Refrain / Troparion 1: Luke 23:42 ('Remember me, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom').
[^6986]: Tone 6 Monday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 2: Supplication for forgiveness of voluntary and involuntary sins.
[^6987]: Tone 6 Monday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 3: Christ enlightening the angelic choirs, invoked to illuminate the eyes of the heart.
[^6988]: Tone 6 Monday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon): The holy passion-bearers healing the wounds of faithful believers.
[^6989]: Tone 6 Monday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Triadikon: Doxology to the Holy Trinity praised by the angelic hosts.
[^6990]: Tone 6 Monday Liturgy, Beatitudes, Theotokion: Deliverance from eternal fire and punishment through the intercession of the Mother of God.
[^6991]: Monday evening opens the liturgical observance of Tuesday, dedicated in the Byzantine rite to the Holy Forerunner and Baptist John.
[^6992]: Melodic model: *Tone 6, Podoben: "Despaired of on account of her life" (Ѿча́ѧннаѧ житїѧ̀ ра́ди)*, corresponding to the Greek automelon *Ἡ ἀπεγνωσμένη διὰ τὸν βίον*.
[^6993]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 1 (concluding from p665): Allusion to the Fall of Adam eating of the forbidden tree in Genesis 3.
[^6994]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 2: Supplication for a contrite spirit and humble heart (Psalm 50:19 / LXX 50:19).
[^6995]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 3: 1 Timothy 2:4 ('God our Savior, Who will have all men to be saved, and to come unto the knowledge of the truth').
[^6996]: Rubric for the Stichera to the Holy Forerunner and Baptist John at Monday Evening Vespers.
[^6997]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 4 (Forerunner 1): Invoking the Forerunner, praised as the greatest born of women (Matthew 11:11; Luke 7:28).
[^6998]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 5 (Forerunner 2): John the Baptist laying his hand upon the head of Christ at the Theophany (Matthew 3:13–17) and preaching the Kingdom of heaven (Matthew 3:2).
[^6999]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 6 (Forerunner 3): The birth of John loosed the barrenness of Elizabeth and the muteness of Zechariah (Luke 1:57–64); John as the Voice of the Word crying in the wilderness (Isaiah 40:3; John 1:23).
[^7000]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Stichera on 'Lord, I have cried', Theotokion: Supplication unto the Virgin for bodily and spiritual chastity.
[^7001]: Rubrics for the evening entrance: the hymn *Phos Hilaron* (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*), the daily Prokeimenon, and the evening prayer *Сподо́би Гдⷭ҇и*.
[^7002]: Rubric for the Penitential Stichera at the Aposticha for Monday Evening Vespers in Tone 6.
[^7003]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 1: Allusion to Matthew 25:12 ('Truly I say unto you, I know you not') at the Second Coming of Christ.
[^7004]: Psalm 122:1–2 (LXX 122:1–2).
[^7005]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 2: Prayer for heartfelt compunction and true repentance before the end of life.
[^7006]: Psalm 122:3–4 (LXX 122:3–4).
[^7007]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon): Commemoration of the holy martyrs as heavenly citizens.
[^7008]: Tone 6 Monday Evening Vespers, Aposticha, Theotokion: The ancient Marian troparion affirming that none who flee unto the Mother of God depart empty-handed.
[^7009]: Concluding rubrics of Monday Evening Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion Prayers, Troparion, Augmented Litany, and Dismissal.
[^7010]: Title and rubric for the Monday Compline Canon (Канѡ́нъ на Повече́рїи) to the Most Holy Theotokos in Tone 6.
[^7011]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1 Heirmos: Song of victory of Moses upon the crossing of the Red Sea (Exodus 14–15).
[^7012]: Liturgical refrain to the Mother of God at the Compline canon.
[^7013]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Bowing down the neck of soul and body in repentance before the Theotokos.
[^7014]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2: The all-immaculate Maiden as the firm refuge of Christians against fleshly passions.
[^7015]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 3: The Theotokos as Guide of light bearing the Noetic Light of Christ.
[^7016]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 4 (concluding from p667): Enlightenment through the commandments of Christ incarnate of the Virgin.
[^7017]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3 Heirmos: 1 Kingdoms 2:1–10 (LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:1–10 / 1 Samuel 2:1–10, Canticle of Hannah).
[^7018]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 1: Deliverance from demonic snares through hope in the Mother of God.
[^7019]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2: The Virgin Mary as the closed gate through which the Lord alone entered and passed (Ezekiel 44:2).
[^7020]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 3: The Mother of God as haven for those tossed upon the stormy sea of life.
[^7021]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 4: Healing of the soul wounded by the fiery arrows of the wicked one (Ephesians 6:16).
[^7022]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2–19 (Canticle of Habakkuk).
[^7023]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 1: The Theotokos as an impregnable wall and intercessor against Gehenna.
[^7024]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 2. Epigraphic note: The 1895 Lviv print exhibits a typographical omission ('вечи́стаѧ' for 'всечи́стаѧ', *all-pure*).
[^7025]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 3: The Virgin as the immaculate receptacle of the divine indwelling.
[^7026]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 4: Cleansing of the soul mired in passions through the prayers of the Maiden.
[^7027]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9–20 (Canticle of Isaiah).
[^7028]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1: Supplication of the sinner mired in falls unto the only undefiled Mother of God.
[^7029]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2: Guiding the darkened soul unto the path of salvation through the light of Christ.
[^7030]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 3: Rending the bonds of sin through the mediation of the Virgin.
[^7031]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 4: The Sovereign Lady of the world as the sole divine refuge of the faithful.
[^7032]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:2–10 (Canticle of Jonah).
[^7033]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 1: Recourse of the wounded sinner to the compassionate Mother of God.
[^7034]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 2: The gate of grace opening the doors of heavenly life and repentance.
[^7035]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 3: The Theotokos as comforter of the soul afflicted by demonic assaults.
[^7036]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 4: Hope and firm foundation in the Mother of God beyond human expectation.
[^7037]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Sessional Hymn after Ode 6: Praising the Virgin Mother as the fortress of the helpless and advocate before God.
[^7038]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:26–56 (Canticle of the Three Holy Children in the Furnace).
[^7039]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 1 (concluding from p669): Deliverance from future eternal torments for all who confess the Virgin as the true Theotokos.
[^7040]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 2: Awakening from spiritual sloth unto the practice of divine virtues.
[^7041]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 3: Deliverance from the voracious noetic serpent seeking the soul's destruction (1 Peter 5:8).
[^7042]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 4: Entreating unceasing pardon and attainment of eternal heavenly goods.
[^7043]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3 and 3 Kingdoms 18:38 (the sacrifice of Elijah consumed by fire).
[^7044]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 1: Making the barren heart fruitful in virtues through the intercession of the Virgin.
[^7045]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Deliverance from passions and demonic snares through the prayers of the Theotokos.
[^7046]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 3: Deliverance from the dread judgment and everlasting fire through Marian patronage.
[^7047]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 4: Deliverance from the portion of the goats at the Last Judgment (Matthew 25:32–33, 41).
[^7048]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9 Heirmos: Luke 1:46–55 (The Magnificat) and 1 Timothy 6:16.
[^7049]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 1: Christ the Helmsman stretching forth the hand of salvation amid the stormy sea of passions.
[^7050]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 2 (conclusion from leaf p670): The Mother of God as the mystical cloud driving away the darkness of sin.
[^7051]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Glory: Prayer to the Theotokos as the gate of light, to shut the entrance of sin and deliver from torment.
[^7052]: Tone 6 Monday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Both now: Exhortation unto the soul to awaken from despondency and abide in prayer under the protection of the Mother of God.
[^7053]: Concluding rubrics of Monday Compline: *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), Trisagion Prayers, Our Father, Dismissal Troparion, and the usual conclusion.
[^7054]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the First Kathisma, Penitential Sessional Hymn 1: Meditation on the Last Judgment, asking mercy of the Holy Trinity (Father, Son, and Holy Spirit).
[^7055]: Psalm 6:2 (LXX 6:1).
[^7056]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the First Kathisma, Penitential Sessional Hymn 2: Pleading for mercy in the valley of weeping (allusion to the Valley of Jehoshaphat, Joel 3:2, 12).
[^7057]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the First Kathisma, Theotokion: The ancient and universal hymn *Open the doors of tender mercy* (*Милосе́рдіѧ две́ри ѿве́рзи на́мъ*).
[^7058]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 1: Exhortation to the soul to flee sin and seek Christ, the Hope of the hopeless and Physician of souls.
[^7059]: Psalm 6:2 (LXX 6:1), repeated as liturgical verse before the second penitential sessional hymn.
[^7060]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 2: Allusion to the Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25:1–13) and prayer for spiritual vigilance and the oil of mercy.
[^7061]: Psalm 67:36 (LXX 67:35), the liturgical verse appointed before the commemoration of the Holy Martyrs.
[^7062]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 3 (Martyrikon): Commemoration of the holy martyrs crowned with victory over the lawless tyrants.
[^7063]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Theotokion: Invocation of the Virgin Mary as the hope of the world and protector of the helpless.
[^7064]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Third Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 1: Commemoration of the preaching of St. John the Forerunner (Matthew 3:3; Isaiah 40:3), calling the soul to straight paths and compunction.
[^7065]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Third Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 2: Christ saving Peter sinking in the storm (Matthew 14:30–31), invoked to deliver the soul from the depth of iniquities through the Forerunner's prayers.
[^7066]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, After the Third Kathisma, Theotokion: The eternal generation of the Word without a mother, and His temporal Incarnation from the Virgin without a father (seedless birth).
[^7067]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1 Heirmos: Exodus 15:1–2 (Canticle of Moses at the Red Sea).
[^7068]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1: The repentant harlot (Luke 7:36–50) as an exemplar of forgiveness through faith and tears.
[^7069]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2: The perilous voyage across the sea of vanity and supplication for rescue from spiritual drowning.
[^7070]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): Praising the martyrs who overthrew the demonic adversary and gained heavenly victory.
[^7071]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2, concluded from leaf p672): The martyrs confounding the pagan philosophers and rhetoricians by the wisdom of the Word.
[^7072]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary as the portal of life closing the gates of death (Ezekiel 44:2).
[^7073]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 1 Heirmos: Exodus 14:21–28 (the drowning of Pharaoh's host in the Red Sea).
[^7074]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Archangel Gabriel announcing the conception of the Forerunner unto Zechariah (Luke 1:11–20).
[^7075]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 1, Troparion 2: The Forerunner growing up in the wilderness (Luke 1:80), invoked to uproot spiritual slothfulness and bring forth fruits of repentance (Matthew 3:8).
[^7076]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 1, Troparion 3: Elizabeth's barrenness loosed by the birth of John (Luke 1:7, 57), prefiguring the healing of spiritual fruitlessness.
[^7077]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 1, Theotokion: Entreaty to the Mother of God to deliver the soul from the snares of the hunter (Psalm 90:3 / LXX 90:3).
[^7078]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3 Heirmos: 1 Samuel 2:2 (LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:2, Canticle of Hannah).
[^7079]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1: Prayer for mercy before the dread tribunal of Christ.
[^7080]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2: The Publican's repentance (Luke 18:13) as a model of contrite prayer from the depth of the soul.
[^7081]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs' outpoured blood putting an end to idolatrous and demonic sacrifices.
[^7082]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs destroying pagan shrines by the power of the Holy Spirit.
[^7083]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary carrying Him Who sustains creation, petitioned to loose the bonds of torment.
[^7084]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 3 Heirmos: 1 Samuel 2:2 (LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:2), establishing the soul upon the rock of faith.
[^7085]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 3, Troparion 1: Supplication to the Forerunner to guide the wanderer back from the path of error.
[^7086]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 3, Troparion 2: Allusion to the axe laid unto the root of the trees (Matthew 3:10; Luke 3:9), warning against spiritual fruitlessness.
[^7087]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 3, Troparion 3: The proximity of the dread day of judgment and prayer for release from sinful burdens.
[^7088]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 3, Theotokion: The Virgin as the throne of the King of kings, raising fallen mankind through her divine Son.
[^7089]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:1–2 (Canticle of Habakkuk).
[^7090]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1: Prayer for the cleansing gift of tears to extinguish the fires of Gehenna.
[^7091]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2: Confession of spiritual insensibility and compulsive self-harm through passions.
[^7092]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs navigating the tempestuous ocean of persecution unto the tranquil haven of the Kingdom.
[^7093]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2, concluded from leaf p674): The holy martyrs as luminous stars dispelling the fog of passions through healing grace.
[^7094]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Theotokion: The prophetic vision of the golden seven-branched lampstand (Exodus 25:31–37; Zechariah 4:2) prefiguring the Theotokos.
[^7095]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2 (Canticle of Habakkuk).
[^7096]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 4, Troparion 1: Allusion to the Good Samaritan parable (Luke 10:30–35), with the soul wounded by spiritual robbers.
[^7097]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 4, Troparion 2: Supplication to St. John the Baptist to overthrow indwelling sin and grant renewal.
[^7098]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 4, Troparion 3: The Forerunner invoked as a safe haven amidst the violent tempest of worldly life.
[^7099]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 4, Theotokion: Joint intercession of the Most Holy Theotokos and St. John the Baptist (the classic Deesis composition) before Christ.
[^7100]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9 (Canticle of Isaiah).
[^7101]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1: The Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25:14–30), the slothful servant pleading not to be cast into the outer darkness.
[^7102]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 2: Allusion to the Parable of the Prodigal Son (Luke 15:11–32) who squandered his sonship in a far country.
[^7103]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs taking the shield of faith (Ephesians 6:16) to quench all the fiery darts of the wicked one.
[^7104]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): Bodily death leading unto eternal and unaging life for the victorious martyrs.
[^7105]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Theotokion: The Theotokos as the closed gate of Ezekiel (Ezekiel 44:2), invoked to unlock the gate of true repentance.
[^7106]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9; the voluntary and impassible self-emptying (kenosis) of the Word (Philippians 2:7).
[^7107]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 5, Troparion 1: The Forerunner as a temple of the Holy Trinity, invoked for protection by those gathered in his sanctuary.
[^7108]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 5, Troparion 2: St. John's ascetic sojourn in the wilderness as a model for drawing near to God.
[^7109]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 5, Troparion 3: St. John baptizing Christ in the Jordan (Matthew 3:13–17), invoked to dry up the torrents of passions.
[^7110]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 5, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary bearing Christ, Who is fair in beauty beyond the sons of men (Psalm 44:3 / LXX 44:2).
[^7111]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:2–10 (Canticle of Jonah).
[^7112]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 1: Pleading not to become a laughingstock for demons at the Last Judgment (Matthew 25:41).
[^7113]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2: Fleeing from the abyss of transgressions unto the fathomless sea of Christ's tender mercies.
[^7114]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): Agricultural metaphor of the martyrs plowing their souls with torments to yield a harvest of eternal life (Psalm 125:5–6 / LXX 125:5–6).
[^7115]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The martyrs wounding Satan through their wounds and becoming healers of passions.
[^7116]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Theotokion: The Theotokos as the living temple of God, making the faithful temples of the Holy Spirit (1 Corinthians 6:19).
[^7117]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 6 Heirmos (concluded from leaf p676): Jonah 2:3, 7 (crying from the belly of the whale).
[^7118]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 6, Troparion 1: St. John as a righteous branch making barren hearts fruitful in praise.
[^7119]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 6, Troparion 2: Romans 16:20 ('The God of peace shall bruise Satan under your feet shortly') and Luke 1:79 ('to guide our feet into the way of peace').
[^7120]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 6, Troparion 3: Invoking the Forerunner to protect his flock against demonic plots and eternal torment.
[^7121]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 6, Theotokion: Deliverance from the ancestral curse of Eve through the Mother of God, bringing forth the fruit of blessing.
[^7122]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:29–30; the prayer of Azariah in the Babylonian furnace.
[^7123]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 1: Appealing to the measureless mercy and patience of Christ for fruits of repentance.
[^7124]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 2: Supplication to Christ the Compassionate for remission of sins and relief from heavy guilt.
[^7125]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): Catalog of martyr torments (fire, beasts, the wheel) endured without denying Christ.
[^7126]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): Romans 8:35–39 (nothing separating the saints from the love of God).
[^7127]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Theotokion: The perpetual virginity of the Theotokos (before, in, and after childbearing, *Aeiparthenos*).
[^7128]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7 Heirmos (concluded from leaf p677): Daniel 3:49–50; the three youths preserved in the dewy furnace.
[^7129]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Troparion 1: St. John as the voice of the Word crying in the wilderness (Isaiah 40:3; John 1:23).
[^7130]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Troparion 2: John 1:29 ('Behold the Lamb of God, which takes away the sin of the world').
[^7131]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Troparion 3: Awakening the soul from irrational passions through the intercession of the Baptist.
[^7132]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Theotokion: The Virgin giving birth to the Lord of hosts before Whom the celestial powers tremble.
[^7133]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3:57–88 (Canticle of the Three Youths).
[^7134]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1: The repentance of King David (Psalm 50 / LXX 50) as a model of seeking swift divine mercy.
[^7135]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Confession of spiritual procrastination and lack of resolve to end wicked deeds.
[^7136]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): Romans 12:1; the martyrs offering themselves as living sacrifices unto Christ.
[^7137]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): Graphic enumeration of physical torments endured by the martyrs for Christ.
[^7138]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Theotokion: Daniel 7:9, 13; the Ancient of Days born as a newborn infant to renew aged human nature.
[^7139]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8 Heirmos (concluded from leaf p678): Daniel 3:16–23; the Three Children defying the tyrant's decree.
[^7140]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Troparion 1: The Theophany at the Jordan (Matthew 3:16–17; Mark 1:10–11; Luke 3:21–22): the Spirit as a dove and the voice of the Father.
[^7141]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Allusion to the burning up of wood, hay, and stubble (1 Corinthians 3:12–15) and relighting the lamp of the soul.
[^7142]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Troparion 3: The Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25:14–30; Luke 19:12–26); begging deliverance from the fire prepared for the unprofitable servant.
[^7143]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Theotokion: Luke 1:52 ('He hath put down the mighty from their seats, and exalted them of low degree; He hath lifted the poor from the dunghill', Psalm 112:7 / LXX 112:7).
[^7144]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9 Heirmos: Luke 1:48 ('For behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed'); the Virgin birth renewing human nature.
[^7145]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 1: Hebrews 4:15; Christ taking on human infirmity to heal and cleanse our nature.
[^7146]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2: Four New Testament paradigms of repentance: the weeping harlot (Luke 7:38), the sighing publican (Luke 18:13), the crying Canaanite woman (Matthew 15:22), and the weeping Apostle Peter (Matthew 26:75).
[^7147]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1, concluded from leaf p679): The martyrs triumphing over Satan, the prince of this world (John 12:31; Ephesians 6:12).
[^7148]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): Earth sanctified by martyr blood and their spirits united with the angelic orders (Hebrews 12:22–24).
[^7149]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Theotokion: Luke 1:28; the angelic salutation and the opening of Eden's gates closed by Eve.
[^7150]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9 Heirmos: The mystical banquet and spiritual hospitality of the Master (Luke 22:15–30).
[^7151]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 1: Prayer to the Forerunner to quiet the storm of passions and direct the soul into the way of peace.
[^7152]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 2: St. John the Baptist as the boundary between the Old and New Testaments (Luke 16:16; Matthew 11:13).
[^7153]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 3: The Forerunner joined unto the heavenly hosts, interceding for those honoring him in his temple.
[^7154]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 4: Zephaniah 1:15 ('That day is a day of wrath, a day of trouble and distress, a day of wasteness and desolation, a day of darkness and gloominess'); prayer for deliverance at the judgment.
[^7155]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Theotokion: The Virgin Mary more honorable than the Cherubim, sanctifying those who praise her day and night.
[^7156]: Matins concluding rubrics: *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the Little Litany, the Exapostilarion, and the usual morning psalms (Psalms 148, 149, 150).
[^7157]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 1: Penitential reflection on the traveler stripped and wounded by robbers (Luke 10:30), crying to Christ the Physician.
[^7158]: Psalm 89:14, 16–17 (LXX 89:14, 16–17), morning verses appointed at Matins Aposticha on weekdays.
[^7159]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 2: Penitential supplication to Christ the Physician of souls and bodies to heal the festering sores of sinful habits and grant the gift of tears.
[^7160]: Psalm 89:17 (LXX 89:17), the concluding liturgical verse of the morning Aposticha.
[^7161]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon): The Holy Passion-bearers invoked as intercessors before Christ Whom the angelic hosts glorify without ceasing.
[^7162]: Tone 6 Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Theotokion: The mystery of the Incarnation, wherein the Virgin Mary bare One of the Holy Trinity in the flesh for human salvation.
[^7163]: Tuesday Matins concluding rubrics: *It is good to give thanks unto the Lord* (Psalm 91:2 / LXX 91:1), Trisagion Prayers, Our Father, Dismissal Troparion, litany of fervent supplication, and the First Hour.
[^7164]: Tone 6 Tuesday Beatitudes: Penitential refrain adapted from the prayer of the Good Thief upon the cross (Luke 23:42).
[^7165]: Tone 6 Tuesday Beatitudes, Troparion 1: Christ receiving the weeping and repentance of the Apostle Peter after his denial (Matthew 26:75; Luke 22:62).
[^7166]: Tone 6 Tuesday Beatitudes, Troparion 2: St. John the Baptist, the preacher of repentance in the wilderness of Judea (Matthew 3:1–2), invoked to inspire genuine compunction.
[^7167]: Tone 6 Tuesday Beatitudes, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon): The holy martyrs enduring the tempestuous waves of tortures and receiving power to heal the diseases of mankind.
[^7168]: Tone 6 Tuesday Beatitudes, Glory: Doxology unto the simple and uncompounded Holy Trinity, praised by the prophets and petitioned through the Forerunner's intercession.
[^7169]: Tone 6 Tuesday Beatitudes, Both now: Supplication to the Mother of God as the steadfast defense of those encompassed by tribulations and worldly passions.
[^7170]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers (opening Wednesday, dedicated to the Precious Cross), Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (*Тридне́внѡ воскре́слъ є҆сѝ* / *Τριήμερος ἀνέστης*).
[^7171]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Sticheron 1 (of the Cross): Cosmic reaction to Christ's Crucifixion: the shaking of the earth and the firm establishing of believers' hearts (Matthew 27:51).
[^7172]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Sticheron 2 (of the Cross): Christ smitten upon the cheek (Matthew 26:67) to overthrow the malicious bite of the ancient serpent that deceived Adam through sight (Genesis 3:6).
[^7173]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Sticheron 3 (of the Cross): The darkening of the sun from the sixth to the ninth hour (Matthew 27:45) witnessing the unjust crucifixion of the Creator.
[^7174]: Rubric directing the singing of Stichera from the Menaion for the Saint commemorated on Wednesday, or these three prosomoia if no Menaion stichera are appointed.
[^7175]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Sticheron 4 (Stavrotheotokion): The Virgin Mary and the virgin disciple John standing beside the Cross (John 19:25–27).
[^7176]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Sticheron 5 (Stavrotheotokion): The Mother of God marveling at the mystery of the Passion, contrasting Christ's pure seedless birth with His ignominious death among evildoers (Isaiah 53:12).
[^7177]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Sticheron 6: Dialogue sticheron wherein the crucified Savior comforts His Mother with the promise of His third-day Resurrection in divine glory (Matthew 16:21).
[^7178]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Stichera on "Lord, I have cried", Glory, Both now (Stavrotheotokion): Fulfillment of the prophecy of the Elder Simeon, as a sword of sorrow pierces the heart of the Virgin Mother (Luke 2:35).
[^7179]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 1 (of the Cross): The Cross of the Lord as the life and defense of the faithful who worship Christ crucified in the flesh.
[^7180]: Psalm 122:1–2 (LXX 122:1–2), appointed as the first liturgical verse before the second sticheron at weekday Vespers Aposticha.
[^7181]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 2 (of the Cross, concluded on leaf p683): The Cross unlocking Paradise unto mankind (Luke 23:43) and delivering from corruption.
[^7182]: Psalm 122:3–4 (LXX 122:3–4), appointed as the second liturgical verse before the Martyrikon at weekday Vespers Aposticha.
[^7183]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha, Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon): The holy martyrs crowned in heaven after enduring manifold torments on earth.
[^7184]: Tone 6 Tuesday Vespers, Aposticha, Glory, Both now (Stavrotheotokion): The All-Blameless Virgin weeping at the sight of the Life of all hanging upon the Tree.
[^7185]: Tuesday Vespers concluding rubrics: *Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart* (Canticle of Simeon, Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion Prayers, Our Father, Dismissal Troparion, litany, and dismissal.
[^7186]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1 Heirmos: Exodus 14:21–29; Israel passing through the Red Sea as on dry land while Pharaoh's army is swallowed up.
[^7187]: Liturgical refrain appointed for the canons to the Theotokos at Compline: *Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us* (*Прест҃а́ѧ Гпⷭ҇же Бцⷣе, спасѝ на́съ*).
[^7188]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Confession of a defiled life and measureless offenses, seeking the help of the Sovereign Lady.
[^7189]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2: Echo of the opening words of the Great Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (*Where shall I begin to bewail the deeds of my miserable life?*), pleading for mercy before the end.
[^7190]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Glory: Meditation on the remembrance of death and the dread tribunal of Christ.
[^7191]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Both now: The devil as the corrupter of good things seeking to devour the soul stripped of virtues (1 Peter 5:8).
[^7192]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3 Heirmos: 1 Samuel 2:2 (LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:2, Canticle of Hannah).
[^7193]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 1: The despairing sinner crying out from the abyss of spiritual pollution.
[^7194]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2 (concluded on leaf p684): Defiling the divine image according to which man was created (Genesis 1:26–27) through high-mindedness and prodigal deeds.
[^7195]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Glory: Lamentation over the darkening of the intellect and the defilement of holy Baptism.
[^7196]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Both now: Creation itself (heaven and earth) grieving over the sinner's boundless transgressions (Isaiah 1:2).
[^7197]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2 (Canticle of Habakkuk); the Church praising Christ as her God, Lord, and Power.
[^7198]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 1: Contrast between the angelic hosts trembling before the divine dominion and the sinner resting in callous fearlessness.
[^7199]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 2: All creation astonished at the contrast between human depravity and the forbearance of God.
[^7200]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Glory: The bodily temple of the Holy Spirit defiled (1 Corinthians 3:16–17; 6:19), and shameful entry into the church of God.
[^7201]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Both now: Supplication not to be cast out from the covering wings of the Mother of God.
[^7202]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9 (Canticle of Isaiah); rising early from the night of sin to seek the divine light of the Word.
[^7203]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1: Prayer to heal passions sown by the enemy like tares among wheat (Matthew 13:25).
[^7204]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2: Typological comparison with Adam, who fell into exile for breaking a single commandment (Genesis 3).
[^7205]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Glory: Typological warning based on Cain, who murdered his brother Abel and was cursed (Genesis 4:8–11), while the sinner slays his own soul without shame.
[^7206]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Both now: Allusion to Esau, who sold his birthright for a single meal (Genesis 25:29–34; Hebrews 12:16), prefiguring the soul enslaved by bodily appetites.
[^7207]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:2–10 (Canticle of Jonah); crying out from the stormy sea of worldly temptations to the haven of salvation.
[^7208]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 1: Confession of total spiritual destitution and plea for the Virgin's swift assistance.
[^7209]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 2: The impending hour of judgment, where hidden deeds of darkness shall be uncovered before Christ's tribunal (2 Corinthians 5:10).
[^7210]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Glory: Eschatological terrors: the river of fire (Daniel 7:10) and the worm that dies not (Mark 9:48).
[^7211]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Both now: Fear of the snares of the hunter (Psalm 90:3 / LXX 90:3) seeking to destroy the soul stripped of the armor of virtues.
[^7212]: Rubric for the triple *Lord, have mercy*, *Glory... Both now...*, and the Sessional Hymn at Compline.
[^7213]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Sessional Hymn: The Virgin Mary celebrated as the unconquerable wall and refuge, who wept beside the Cross of her Son.
[^7214]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:49–50; the Angel of the Lord descending into the furnace and making the middle of the flame like a moist whistling wind.
[^7215]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 1: Allusion to Nebuchadnezzar heating the Babylonian furnace seven times more (Daniel 3:19), applied to the enemy inflaming the heart with carnal passions.
[^7216]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 2 (concluded on leaf p686): Sinking in the deep mire of sins (Psalm 68:3 / LXX 68:2) and imploring the Sovereign Lady's rescuing hand.
[^7217]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Glory: Exhortation unto the contrite soul to cast itself before the Mother of the Judge rather than fall into despair.
[^7218]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Both now: Supplication to the All-Pure to guide the darkened mind into the sweet haven of dispassion (*apatheia*).
[^7219]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8 Heirmos: 1 Kings 18:38 (LXX 3 Kingdoms 18:38; the fire from heaven consuming Elijah's sacrifice and licking up the water) and Daniel 3:50.
[^7220]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 1: The Virgin bearing the Incarnate Word in her arms, invoked to quench the furnace of passions with tears.
[^7221]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 2: Confession of the dread fear of death and judgment juxtaposed with habitual unrepentance.
[^7222]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Glory: Pleading for the charism of unceasing sighs and copious tears of repentance to wash away the scars of sin.
[^7223]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Both now: Confession of unparalleled sins before the Mother of God, begging her to turn her Son's wrath into tender mercy.
[^7224]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9 Heirmos: 1 Timothy 6:16 and John 1:18 (no man having seen God at any time), made visible in the flesh through the Incarnation from the Virgin.
[^7225]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 1: The traditional patristic teaching on the efficacy and boldness of a mother's intercession before her Son.
[^7226]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 2 (concluded on leaf p687): The Virgin mobilizing the entire celestial hierarchy (Archangels, Apostles, Prophets, Martyrs, Hierarchs) in joint supplication for sinners.
[^7227]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Glory: Prayer for maternal protection at the dreadful hour of the soul's departure (*in articulo mortis*) against demonic accusers (aerial toll-houses).
[^7228]: Tone 6 Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Both now: Supplication to be placed among the sheep on the right hand at the Last Judgment (Matthew 25:33).
[^7229]: Tuesday Compline concluding rubrics: *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), Trisagion Prayers, Our Father, Dismissal Troparion, and the usual conclusion.
[^7230]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 1 (of the Cross): Sanctification and healing bestowed through the veneration of the Cross.
[^7231]: Psalm 98:5 (LXX 98:5), the canonical liturgical verse appointed before the veneration of the Precious Cross.
[^7232]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 2 (of the Cross): The setting up of the Cross shaking the foundations of death and compelling hades to release the captives it had swallowed.
[^7233]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the First Kathisma, Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "O hope of the world" (*Наде́жда мíра* / *Ἡ ἐλπὶς τοῦ κόσμου*): The Virgin lamenting beside the Cross at the sight of the Judge condemned by the lawless.
[^7234]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 1 (of the Cross): Psalm 131:7 (LXX 131:7; *We will worship at the place where His feet stood*), celebrating liberation from passions through the wood of the Cross.
[^7235]: Psalm 73:12 (LXX 73:12), the traditional liturgical verse chanted before hymns commemorating Christ's victory on the Cross in the midst of the earth (Golgotha).
[^7236]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 2 (of the Cross): Typological reproach against the ungrateful people who passed through the Red Sea (Exodus 14) and sucked honey from the rock (Deuteronomy 32:13; Psalm 80:17 / LXX 80:16), yet gave Christ gall and vinegar upon the Cross (Psalm 68:22 / LXX 68:21; Matthew 27:34).
[^7237]: Psalm 67:36 (LXX 67:35), the liturgical verse appointed before the commemoration of the Holy Martyrs.
[^7238]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 3 (Martyrikon): The steadfast witness of the holy martyrs confounding earthly tyrants and demonic powers before the angelic hosts.
[^7239]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Second Kathisma, Stavrotheotokion: The Virgin Mother beholding her Son hanging upon the Tree, wounded by the sword of Simeon's prophecy (Luke 2:35).
[^7240]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Third Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 1 (of the Cross), Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (*Всѐ ѿло́жше* / *Ὅλην ἀποθέмеνοι*): The cosmic convulsions attending the Crucifixion (Matthew 27:45, 51; Luke 23:44–45).
[^7241]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Third Kathisma, Sessional Hymn 2 (of the Cross): The theological parallel between the tree of knowledge in Eden bringing death to Adam and the Tree of the Cross on Calvary bringing eternal life.
[^7242]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, After the Third Kathisma, Stavrotheotokion: The Ever-Virgin Maiden weeping at the elevation of her Son upon the Cross for the salvation of mankind.
[^7243]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Precious Cross by Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the alphabetical acrostic: *Thou savest me, O Savior, nailed upon the Wood* (*Спаса́еши мѧ̀ Спа́се, пригвожде́сѧ на дре́вѣ*). Ode 1 Heirmos: Exodus 14:21–29; Israel passing through the Red Sea on dry land (concluded on leaf p689).
[^7244]: Liturgical refrain appointed for the Canon of the Cross: *Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross and Crucifixion* (*Сла́ва Гдⷭ҇и крестꙋ̀ твоемꙋ̀ и҆ распѧ́тїю*).
[^7245]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Christ stretching out His hands upon the Cross to embrace all scattered mankind and reconcile them to the Father (John 12:32).
[^7246]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 1, Troparion 2: The Good Shepherd seeking the lost sheep and restoring it unto the ninety-nine (Luke 15:4–7), fulfilled through the sacrifice of the Lamb of God (John 1:29).
[^7247]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 1, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The martyrs willingly embracing physical death on earth to obtain heavenly and unaging life.
[^7248]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 1, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): The passion-bearers enduring unjust human courts, whose unjust verdicts work divine justification before God.
[^7249]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 1, Stavrotheotokion: Christ suffering in the flesh to grant dispassion to fallen humanity, while creation shakes and His Mother laments.
[^7250]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1 Heirmos: The same heirmos as the Canon of the Cross (*When Israel traversed the deep on foot*).
[^7251]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1: The Creator Who stretched out the heavens (Psalm 103:2 / LXX 103:2; Isaiah 40:22) taking flesh from the Virgin, revealing her as an earthly heaven.
[^7252]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2: Supplication to the Theotokos to intercede for mercy at the dread Day of Judgment.
[^7253]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 3: Christ as the Sun of Righteousness (Malachi 4:2) rising from the virginal womb to enlighten the entire world.
[^7254]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 4: Prayer to dispel the fog of sinful thoughts through the luminous commandments of the Incarnate Word.
[^7255]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 3 Heirmos: 1 Samuel 2:2 (LXX 1 Kingdoms 2:2, Canticle of Hannah).
[^7256]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 3, Troparion 1: The erection of the Cross upon earth overturning idolatrous deception and confirming the hearts of the faithful.
[^7257]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 3, Troparion 2: The unjust mob condemning the only Just One (Acts 3:14) Who justifies mankind and rescues them from Satan's grasp.
[^7258]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 3, Troparion 3 (Martyrikon 1): The demonic deceiver frustrated by the unyielding endurance of the holy martyrs, who become partakers of divine glory.
[^7259]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 3, Troparion 4 (Martyrikon 2): Physical beauty mutilated by savage wounds, while the spiritual beauty of the soul shines forth brightly through the Holy Spirit.
[^7260]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 3, Stavrotheotokion: The Virgin mourning the contrast between Christ's divine beauty, fairer than the sons of men (Psalm 44:3 / LXX 44:2), and His disfigurement upon the Cross (Isaiah 53:2–3).
[^7261]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 1: The Virgin carrying in her arms Him Who sustains the universe by the word of His power (Hebrews 1:3), invoked to rescue believers from the devil's grip.
[^7262]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2: Entreaty to the all-blameless Lady for deliverance from demonic delusions, unjust adversaries, and pestilential afflictions.
[^7263]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 3: Fulfillment of the Magnificat prophecy (Luke 1:48), praising her who gave birth in time to the timeless Word while remaining an undefiled Virgin.
[^7264]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 4: Prayer of the unprofitable servant (Luke 17:10) for deliverance from condemnation at the dread Second Coming of Christ.
[^7265]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2 (Canticle of Habakkuk); the Church rejoicing in Christ her Lord, God, and Strength.
[^7266]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 4, Troparion 1: The physical sun darkened in terror at the crucifixion of the spiritual and unsetting Sun (Matthew 27:45), delivering creation from spiritual darkness.
[^7267]: Tone 6 Wednesday Matins, Canon of the Cross, Ode 4, Troparion 2 (concluded on leaf p691): The hands of the Savior bound by lawless men, loosing all who were held fast in the unbreakable bonds of sin and death.
[^7268]: Physical leaf p691 completes the troparion of Ode 4 from p690: "...were loosed, and the enemy was bound, and deception was destroyed."
[^7269]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of Ode 4, drawing typologically upon the drowning of Pharaoh in the Red Sea (Exodus 14:28).
[^7270]: Troparion 4 (Martyrikon) of Ode 4, commemorating the ascent of the holy martyrs on spiritual wings unto the unwaning light.
[^7271]: Theotokion of Ode 4, meditating upon Simeon's prophecy of the sword piercing the soul of the Virgin Mother at the Passion of her Son (Luke 2:35).
[^7272]: Rubric initiating the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 6, Ode 4, sung to the same heirmos.
[^7273]: Troparion 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, honoring the Virgin as the living temple of God.
[^7274]: Troparion 2 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, praising the Theotokos as the spiritual door, the divine paradise, the holy place of sanctification, and the beauty of Jacob (Psalm 46:5 [LXX]).
[^7275]: Troparion 3 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, hailing the Virgin as the calm haven of those who invoke her in faith and love.
[^7276]: Troparion 4 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, entreating the all-pure Mary, the spacious dwelling-place of God, to cleanse the soul's defilement.
[^7277]: Ode 5. Heirmos: "With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate the souls of those who lovingly rise early unto Thee..." Cf. Isaiah 26:9.
[^7278]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5, contrasting Christ voluntarily stripped and nailed naked upon the Cross with His divine power clothing the heavens with clouds (Psalm 103:2 [LXX]).
[^7279]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5, celebrating Christ as the True Vine (John 15:1) pouring forth sweet new wine to destroy demonic intoxication.
[^7280]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of Ode 5, depicting the holy martyrs adorned with wounds and crowns before the incarnate God.
[^7281]: Physical leaf p692 begins with Troparion 4 (Martyrikon) of Ode 5, praising the passion-bearers who endured the tempest of evils for the sake of heavenly glory.
[^7282]: Theotokion of Ode 5, recording the Mother of God contrasting her painless childbirth with the bitter agony of beholding her Son crucified.
[^7283]: Troparion 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, glorifying the Creator and Lord of all Who through mercy took flesh from the Virgin.
[^7284]: Troparion 2 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, recalling the prophetic conception and perpetual virginity of the Theotokos (Isaiah 7:14).
[^7285]: Troparion 3 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, praying to Mary to tear up the bond of sins by the lance of Christ (Colossians 2:14).
[^7286]: Troparion 4 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, imploring the Virgin to shatter the fetters of sin as the hope of the desperate.
[^7287]: Ode 6. Heirmos: "Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry aloud unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One." Cf. Jonah 2:3-7.
[^7288]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6, praising Christ Who was crowned with thorns to prune away the thorns of human passions and slew the serpent by His pierced side.
[^7289]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6, worshiping the voluntary long-suffering of Christ, Who was lifted up on the Cross to liberate mankind from bitter slavery.
[^7290]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of Ode 6, commemorating the holy martyrs who through the power of the Cross destroyed the demonic fortress and entered the heavenly city.
[^7291]: Physical leaf p693 begins with Troparion 4 (Martyrikon) of Ode 6, honoring the saints who ascended the height of martyrdom and cast down the pride of the enemy.
[^7292]: Theotokion of Ode 6, expressing the Mother's lamentation seeing the Radiance of the Father (Hebrews 1:3) lifted upon the Cross.
[^7293]: Troparion 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, noting that Jesus Who is an unconsuming fire preserved the Virgin's womb unburned.
[^7294]: Troparion 2 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, asking the Virgin, the comeliness of the Angels, to banish demonic delusions.
[^7295]: Troparion 3 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, on the seedless incarnation of the Only-begotten Son. The physical print reads 'поврежде́нно', an epigraphic typographical erratum for 'неврежде́нно' (unharmed/incorrupt), corrected contextually in translation.
[^7296]: Troparion 4 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6, praying for the cleansing of manifold sins through the boundless mercy of the Theotokos.
[^7297]: Ode 7. Heirmos: "The Angel made the furnace dew-bearing for the venerable children, but the command of God burning the Chaldeans..." Cf. Daniel 3:49-50.
[^7298]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7, singing of Christ Who through His buffeting struck down the serpent's pride.
[^7299]: Troparion 2 of Ode 7, contrasting Adam's death from the tree in Eden with the immortal life granted by Christ upon the tree of the Cross.
[^7300]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of Ode 7, honoring the steadfastness of the holy martyrs upon the rock of faith.
[^7301]: Physical leaf p694 begins by completing Troparion 4 (Martyrikon) of Ode 7: "...more radiantly than the sun, and ye destroyed all the powers of darkness, singing unto Christ: Blessed is the God of our fathers."
[^7302]: Theotokion of Ode 7, the Virgin weeping at the sight of creation trembling before the long-suffering Crucified God.
[^7303]: Troparion 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, the paradox of the Uncircumscribed God sitting in the bosom of the Father and resting circumscribed in the Virgin's womb.
[^7304]: Troparion 2 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, pleading for unceasing intercession before God the Lover of mankind.
[^7305]: Troparion 3 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, blessing the Theotokos who brought forth the Lord Who renews aging humanity.
[^7306]: Troparion 4 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, hailing the Mother of God as the secure haven from worldly storms.
[^7307]: Ode 8. Heirmos: "From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the holy ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one and the water..." Cf. 1 Kings 18:38 [LXX: 3 Kings 18:38], Daniel 3:50.
[^7308]: Troparion 1 of Ode 8, praising the humble exaltation of Christ on the Tree, humbling the proud serpent and lifting up fallen Adam.
[^7309]: Troparion 2 of Ode 8, meditating upon the bitter gall tasted by Christ to sweeten the ancient bitterness of sin.
[^7310]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of Ode 8, honoring the martyrs who refused to bow to idols and were sacrificed as pure lambs.
[^7311]: Troparion 4 (Martyrikon) of Ode 8, extolling the martyrs as living temples of the Holy Spirit who tore down the pagan shrines.
[^7312]: Physical leaf p695 begins with the Theotokion of Ode 8, typologically interpreting Jacob's ladder (Genesis 28:12) as the Virgin Mary leading mankind to heaven.
[^7313]: Troparion 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, reiterating the true confession of the Theotokos with the Archangel Gabriel's salutation (Luke 1:28).
[^7314]: Troparion 2 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, identifying David's prophetic harp and the Ark of the Covenant as types of the Virgin bearing God in the flesh.
[^7315]: Troparion 3 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, invoking the unwedded Maiden as the haven and hope of Christians.
[^7316]: Troparion 4 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8, praying for placement among the righteous sheep at the Last Judgment (Matthew 25:33).
[^7317]: Ode 9. Heirmos: "It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O All-Pure, the Word Incarnate appeared unto men..."
[^7318]: Troparion 1 of Ode 9, praising the Lord of ages Who was lifted up at midday to heal Adam's midday transgression in Eden.
[^7319]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9, celebrating the planting of the Cross, which mortally wounded Satan and loosed the prisoners from corruption.
[^7320]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of Ode 9, honoring the holy martyrs whose sacrifice unites earth with the choir of heavenly angels.
[^7321]: Physical leaf p696 begins by completing Troparion 4 (Martyrikon) of Ode 9, celebrating the deification by grace of the holy martyrs dwelling in the heavenly mansions.
[^7322]: Theotokion of Ode 9, the Virgin expressing her sorrow at seeing her Son crucified, before Whom creation trembled in fear.
[^7323]: Troparion 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, confessing the unoriginate Word becoming the Son of the Virgin by the Father's good pleasure and the Spirit's descent.
[^7324]: Troparion 2 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, exhorting the soul to shake off spiritual sloth under the watchful vigilance of the Mother of God.
[^7325]: Troparion 3 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, petitioning the Virgin for light to dispel the darkness of sinful thoughts.
[^7326]: Troparion 4 of the Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9, imploring the Theotokos to open the gates of light and guide life into the haven of God's commandments.
[^7327]: Rubric directing the hymn 'It is truly meet' (Axion Estin), litany, Exapostilarion, and the usual morning psalms (Lauds / Praises).
[^7328]: Sticheron 1 of the Cross at the Aposticha, Tone 6, placing hope in the Cross and praying against the pride of those who deny Christ's true divinity and humanity.
[^7329]: Aposticha Verse 1: "We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad: ... And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their sons." Psalm 89:14, 16 [LXX].
