[^7330]: Sticheron 2 of the Cross at the Aposticha, Tone 6, proclaiming the defeat and trampling down of the devil through the power of the Crucified Christ.
[^7331]: Aposticha Verse 2: "And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and direct Thou the works of our hands upon us, yea, direct Thou the work of our hands." Psalm 89:17 [LXX].
[^7332]: Physical leaf p697 begins with Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon) at the Aposticha, praising the joyful memory and intercessory boldness of the martyrs.
[^7333]: Melodic model heading for the Stavrotheotokion: Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше).
[^7334]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha, depicting the Mother's weeping and lamentation at the injustice of the Jewish council and Pilate's judgment seat.
[^7335]: Rubric governing the conclusion of Matins: 'It is good to give thanks' (Psalm 91:2 [LXX]), Trisagion prayers, Troparion, litany, and dismissal.
[^7336]: Refrain of the Beatitudes for Wednesday, Tone 6, adapted from the Good Thief's cry (Luke 23:42).
[^7337]: Troparion 1 of the Beatitudes, praising Christ Who voluntarily ascended the Tree to restore those sunken in sin.
[^7338]: Troparion 2 of the Beatitudes, recalling the cosmic convulsions of earth and sun at the voluntary Passion of the Sun of Righteousness.
[^7339]: Troparion 3 (Martyrikon) of the Beatitudes, honoring the deification of the martyrs who shared in the Savior's sufferings.
[^7340]: Troparion 4 (Triadikon) of the Beatitudes, invoking Christ, One of the Holy Trinity, Who willingly endured crucifixion.
[^7341]: Troparion 5 (Theotokion) of the Beatitudes, the Virgin Mother magnifying her suffering Son beside the Cross.
[^7342]: Rubric indicating Wednesday evening Vespers, which initiates the liturgical commemoration of Thursday (dedicated to the Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas).
[^7343]: Rubric governing the Stichera on "Lord, I have cried" to the Holy Apostles, Tone 6.
[^7344]: Melodic model: Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ воскре́слъ є҆сѝ).
[^7345]: Physical leaf p698 begins by completing Sticheron 1 to the Holy Apostles from p697, commemorating their ministry as theologians, eye-witnesses, and healers of mankind.
[^7346]: Sticheron 2 to the Holy Apostles, honoring the disciples as the refuge of souls and the expulsion of demonic powers.
[^7347]: Sticheron 3 to the Holy Apostles, petitioning for deliverance from all adversities, demonic injury, and wicked captivity.
[^7348]: Rubric introducing three additional stichera to Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker of Myra in Lycia, sung to the same melody and tone.
[^7349]: Sticheron 4 (Nicholas 1), praying to the holy hierarch to enlighten spiritual sight to behold the divine Giver of light.
[^7350]: Sticheron 5 (Nicholas 2), imploring Saint Nicholas for protection from afflictions and men of blood (Psalm 25:9 [LXX]).
[^7351]: Sticheron 6 (Nicholas 3), extolling Saint Nicholas as a stormless haven, an unshakeable fortress, a tower of strength, and a guide of repentance.
[^7352]: Theotokion on "Lord, I have cried," beseeching the Virgin's defense against the envious adversary seeking to devour the flock.
[^7353]: Rubric directing the evening hymn 'O Gladsome Radiance' (Phos Hilaron), daily Prokimenon, and evening prayer 'Vouchsafe, O Lord'.
[^7354]: Sticheron 1 to the Holy Apostles at the Aposticha, Tone 6, contrasting the physical fishing nets of the Apostles with their theological ascent to proclaim the ageless Son of God.
[^7355]: Aposticha Verse 1: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven: ... So our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have compassion on us." Psalm 122:1-2 [LXX].
[^7356]: Sticheron 2 to the Holy Apostles at the Aposticha, Tone 6, recalling the disciples crying out during the tempest on the Sea of Galilee (Matthew 8:25) as a model of prayer.
[^7357]: Aposticha Verse 2: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: ... For our soul is exceedingly filled with the reproach of the prosperous, and with the contempt of the proud." Psalm 122:3-4 [LXX].
[^7358]: Physical leaf p699 begins with Sticheron 3 (Martyrikon) at the Aposticha, celebrating the Cross as the invincible weapon of the martyrs.
[^7359]: Theotokion at the Aposticha, imploring the Mother of God for protection in the hour of her Son's righteous judgment.
[^7360]: Rubric for the Song of Simeon: 'Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace' (Luke 2:29).
[^7361]: Rubric initiating the Penitential Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos at Compline on Wednesday evening, Tone 6, bearing the acrostic: "Most Holy, receive the lamentation of John" (Прест҃а́ѧ, прїимѝ І҆ѡа́нна рыда́нїе).
[^7362]: Ode 1. Heirmos: "He was my Helper and Protector unto salvation, this is my God, and I will glorify Him..." Exodus 15:2.
[^7363]: Refrain for the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline: "Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us."
[^7364]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1, voicing deep contrition and pleading for the compassion of the Mother of God.
[^7365]: Troparion 2 of Ode 1, summoning the soul to arise from dishonor and seek deliverance from passions through the Theotokos.
[^7366]: Troparion 3 of Ode 1, calling upon the Virgin to stretch forth a hand of rescue from the tempest of sins.
[^7367]: Troparion 4 of Ode 1, asking the Mother of God to lighten the overwhelming weight of transgressions.
[^7368]: Ode 3. Heirmos: "Establish upon the rock of Thy commandments, O Lord, my shaken heart, for Thou alone art holy and Lord." Cf. 1 Samuel 2:2 [LXX: 1 Kings 2:2].
[^7369]: Troparion 1 of Ode 3, praying for spiritual renewal and loosening of the chains of aging sins.
[^7370]: Troparion 2 of Ode 3, entreating the Theotokos to cleanse the soul from the defiling mire of passion and make it a pure temple.
[^7371]: Physical leaf p700 begins by completing Troparion 3 of Ode 3, admonishing the soul to look up unto the Virgin Maiden for salvation.
[^7372]: Troparion 4 of Ode 3, approaching the Sovereign Lady in brokenness and contrite supplication.
[^7373]: Ode 4. Heirmos: "The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid, how Thou wast to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men..." Habakkuk 3:2.
[^7374]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4, anticipating the strict divine tribunal and praying for the Mother's defense.
[^7375]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4, pleading for purification and whiteness like snow (Psalm 50:9 [LXX]) through the Virgin's intercession.
[^7376]: Troparion 3 of Ode 4, crying out from the tight bonds of sin for the Virgin's guiding hand toward salvation.
[^7377]: Troparion 4 of Ode 4, petitioning for deliverance from outer darkness and Gehenna by the power of the Mother of the All-Good Lord.
[^7378]: Ode 5. Heirmos: "Rising early from the night, O Lover of mankind, enlighten me, I pray, and guide me in Thy commandments..." Cf. Isaiah 26:9.
[^7379]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5, imploring the Theotokos to stand by the sinner in the dreadful day of judgment.
[^7380]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5, describing the assault and mocking laughter of the demonic enemy when the soul is stripped of the Virgin's protective veil.
[^7381]: Troparion 3 of Ode 5, begging the Immaculate Virgin to raise the fallen soul and remove the crushing load of sins.
[^7382]: Concluding stanza of physical leaf p700, Troparion 4 of Ode 5, offering prayer to the Virgin; the incomplete sentence is concluded on leaf p701 at the opening of Cohort 71.
[^7383]: Syntactic continuation from leaf p700: "...from my defiled and wretched and unclean lips: have compassion on me by thy prayer, I pray thee."
[^7384]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos at Compline, Tone 6 (Jonah 2:2).
[^7385]: The division between the sheep and the goats at the Last Judgment: "and deliver me from the part on the left" (шꙋ́їѧ ча́сти и҆зба́ви мѧ̀; Matt. 25:33).
[^7386]: Lit. "the lot of the goats" (кѡ́злищъ ча́сть; Matt. 25:32-33).
[^7387]: 'Passionate nature' or 'passibility' of the flesh (пло́ти моеѧ̀ стра́стное).
[^7388]: Slavonic 'неразꙋ́мный а҆́зъ' (senseless/foolish as I am).
[^7389]: Rubric concluding Ode 6 at Compline: Trisagion prayers, Lord, have mercy (thrice), Glory... both now.
[^7390]: Sessional Hymn (Сѣда́ленъ) of the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline, Tone 6.
[^7391]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline, Tone 6 (Dan. 3:29-30 LXX / Song of the Three Holy Children).
[^7392]: First troparion of Ode 7 of the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline.
[^7393]: Second troparion of Ode 7.
[^7394]: Doxasticon of Ode 7.
[^7395]: Both now (Theotokion) of Ode 7.
[^7396]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline, Tone 6 (Dan. 3:57-88 LXX / Song of the Three Children).
[^7397]: First troparion of Ode 8.
[^7398]: Second troparion of Ode 8.
[^7399]: Doxasticon of Ode 8.
[^7400]: Both now (Theotokion) of Ode 8.
[^7401]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline, Tone 6.
[^7402]: First troparion of Ode 9.
[^7403]: Second troparion of Ode 9.
[^7404]: Doxasticon of Ode 9; the sentence continues across the folio boundary onto leaf p703.
[^7405]: Continuation of the Doxasticon of Ode 9 from leaf p702: "...praying, Thy Mother, the Bride of God."
[^7406]: Both now (Theotokion) concluding Ode 9 of the Canon to the Theotokos at Compline.
[^7407]: Rubrical dismissal formula for Compline: "It is truly meet. Trisagion; and after Our Father: Troparion and the rest as usual."
[^7408]: Opening rubric for the Thursday Matins office (Въ Четверто́къ Оу҆́тра).
[^7409]: Rubric preceding the Sessional Hymns after the 1st Kathisma at Matins, Tone 6.
[^7410]: First Sessional Hymn to the Holy Apostles after the 1st Kathisma.
[^7411]: Prokeimenon verse to the Apostles: Ps. 18:5 LXX (19:4 MT).
[^7412]: Second Sessional Hymn to the Holy Apostles after the 1st Kathisma.
[^7413]: Theotokion after the 1st Kathisma.
[^7414]: Rubric preceding the Sessional Hymns after the 2nd Kathisma at Matins, Tone 6.
[^7415]: First Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma. The 1895 Lviv edition contains a typographical error in the word 'gentiles/nations' ('ꙗ҆зки́ки' for 'ꙗ҆зы́ки').
[^7416]: Verse: Ps. 88:6 LXX (89:5 MT).
[^7417]: Second Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma, addressed to the twelve Apostles (двоена́десатица а҆пⷭ҇лъ).
[^7418]: Verse: Ps. 67:36 LXX (68:35 MT).
[^7419]: Martyrikon (Мч҃ениченъ) Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma.
[^7420]: Theotokion after the 2nd Kathisma.
[^7421]: Rubric preceding the Sessional Hymns after the 3rd Kathisma at Matins, Tone 6.
[^7422]: Canonical Ruthenian melodic model (Подо́бенъ): Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше).
[^7423]: First Sessional Hymn to the Apostles after the 3rd Kathisma.
[^7424]: Second Sessional Hymn to the Apostles after the 3rd Kathisma.
[^7425]: Theotokion after the 3rd Kathisma.
[^7426]: Title and authorial inscription of the primary Canon to the Holy Apostles, composed by St. Theophanes the Branded (Ѳеофа́нъ Начерта́нный).
[^7427]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Apostles, Tone 6 (Exod. 15:1-19).
[^7428]: Canonical liturgical refrain to the Holy Apostles.
[^7429]: First troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Apostles.
[^7430]: Second troparion of Ode 1 (referencing the "prince of the air", Eph. 2:2).
[^7431]: Third troparion of Ode 1.
[^7432]: Theotokion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Apostles.
[^7433]: Rubric introducing the Second Canon to St. Nicholas of Myra.
[^7434]: Authorial inscription and alphabetic/metrical acrostic: "The sixth song, O Nicholas, receive" (Шесто́е пѣ́нїе Нїко́лае, прїимѝ), composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer.
[^7435]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the Canon to St. Nicholas, Tone 6 (Exod. 15:1-19).
[^7436]: First troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon to St. Nicholas; continues across the folio boundary onto leaf p705.
[^7437]: Continuation of the first troparion of Ode 1 to St. Nicholas from leaf p704: "...in concord hymn thee, drive away all evil sorrow of their heart by thine intercession."
[^7438]: Second troparion of Ode 1 to St. Nicholas.
[^7439]: Third troparion of Ode 1 to St. Nicholas.
[^7440]: Theotokion of Ode 1 of the Canon to St. Nicholas.
[^7441]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the Canon to the Apostles, Tone 6 (1 Sam. 2:2 LXX [1 Kings]).
[^7442]: First troparion of Ode 3 of the Canon to the Apostles (referencing Pentecost).
[^7443]: Second troparion of Ode 3 to the Apostles.
[^7444]: Third troparion of Ode 3 to the Apostles.
[^7445]: Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Canon to the Apostles.
[^7446]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the Canon to St. Nicholas, Tone 6.
[^7447]: First troparion of Ode 3 to St. Nicholas.
[^7448]: Second troparion of Ode 3 to St. Nicholas.
[^7449]: Third troparion of Ode 3 to St. Nicholas.
[^7450]: Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Canon to St. Nicholas.

[^7632]: Rubric indicating Tone and Heirmos are identical to the preceding Canon.
[^7633]: First troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos; continues across the folio boundary onto leaf p720.
[^7634]: Syntactic continuation of Troparion 1 of Ode 1 from leaf p719: "...inclined unto thy supplications; thou hast strength and dominion, an unconquerable intercession, O Lady Birthgiver of God: therefore save thy servant."
[^7635]: Second troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos.
[^7636]: Third troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos.
[^7637]: Fourth troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos.
[^7638]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the Canon of the Cross, Tone 6.
[^7639]: First troparion of Ode 3 of the Canon of the Cross.
[^7640]: Second troparion of Ode 3 of the Canon of the Cross.
[^7641]: First Martyrikon troparion of Ode 3.
[^7642]: Second Martyrikon troparion of Ode 3.
[^7643]: Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Canon of the Cross.
[^7644]: Liturgical designation for the continuation of the second weekday Matins canon to the Theotokos in Ode 3, repeating the heirmos of the preceding Cross canon (*Beholding Thee Who suspended all the earth...*).
[^7645]: Troparion petitioning the Virgin Mother to deliver from darkness and the unquenchable flame of fire.
[^7646]: Troparion of personal repentance pleading for the healing of spiritual scabs and wounds.
[^7647]: Troparion invoking the sovereign intercession of the Virgin against slavery to sin.
[^7648]: Concluding troparion of Ode 3 in the Theotokos canon.
[^7649]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the Cross Canon citing Hab. 3:2–3.
[^7650]: Troparion contrasting the reaching out for the forbidden fruit in Eden with Christ's arms stretched upon the Cross. Slavonic *сказаннымъ* translates Greek *τοῖς πιστοῖς* / *ῥηθεῖσι* (those indicated / the faithful).
[^7651]: Troparion on the gall, suffering, and voluntary death of Christ as the source of eternal sweetness and life.
[^7652]: First martyrikon of Ode 4 praising the passion-bearers who quenched worldly persecution through divine love.
[^7653]: Second martyrikon commemorating the martyrs as true friends of the universal King.
[^7654]: Stavrotheotokion recording the maternal lament of the Virgin before the suffering of her Son.
[^7655]: Rubric indicating that the Theotokos canon in Ode 4 shares the heirmos of the Cross canon (*Foreseeing Thy divine emptying...*).
[^7656]: Opening troparion of Ode 4 of the Theotokos canon, running across the folio turn onto Leaf p722.
[^7657]: Completion of the opening troparion of Ode 4 from Leaf p721.
[^7658]: Troparion petitioning the Theotokos for safe pilotage across the storms of sin and passions.
[^7659]: Prayer for an easy passing and angelic deliverance at the dread hour of death and soul-body separation.
[^7660]: Concluding troparion of Ode 4 in the Theotokos canon.
[^7661]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the Cross Canon based on Isa. 26:9.
[^7662]: Troparion meditating on Christ stripped of bodily beauty on Golgotha (Isa. 53:2) to restore divine beauty unto man.
[^7663]: Troparion on the crucifixion reversing Adam's transgression and reopening the cherubim-guarded gates of Paradise (Gen. 3:24).
[^7664]: First martyrikon of Ode 5 praising the physical dismemberment endured by the martyrs.
[^7665]: Second martyrikon extolling the spiritual victory of Christlike humility over demonic pride.
[^7666]: Stavrotheotokion referencing the rod from the stem of Jesse (Isa. 11:1) and the wood of the Cross.
[^7667]: Rubric indicating the Theotokos canon in Ode 5 repeats the heirmos *Unto Thee I rise early...*
[^7668]: Troparion venerating the icon of the Mother of God, running over onto Leaf p723.
[^7669]: Completion of Troparion 1 of Ode 5 from Leaf p722, confessing the intercessory grace mediated through the veneration of the icon of the Theotokos.
[^7670]: Troparion appealing to the tender compassions of the Ever-Virgin for remission of debts.
[^7671]: Troparion for deliverance from despondency, emotional tribulation, and demonic assaults.
[^7672]: Concluding troparion of Ode 5 of the Theotokos canon.
[^7673]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the Cross Canon citing Jonah 2:1–9.
[^7674]: Troparion interpreting the crossed hands of the Patriarch Jacob over Ephraim and Manasseh (Gen. 48:13–14) as a prototype of the Cross.
[^7675]: Typological troparion on the brazen serpent raised in the wilderness (Num. 21:8–9; Jn. 3:14).
[^7676]: First martyrikon of Ode 6 praising the dispassionate fortitude of the martyrs during torture.
[^7677]: Second martyrikon of Ode 6, continuing across the leaf turn onto Leaf p724.
[^7678]: Completion of Martyrikon 2 of Ode 6 from Leaf p723.
[^7679]: Stavrotheotokion of Ode 6 capturing the lamentation of the Virgin over the paradox of the immortal God tasting death.
[^7680]: Rubric indicating the Theotokos canon in Ode 6 repeats the heirmos *Jonah was caught...*
[^7681]: Troparion pleading for the deliverance of soul and body from demonic entrapment.
[^7682]: Troparion invoking the Virgin Mother to disperse spiritual darkness with the light of repentance.
[^7683]: Troparion addressing the Theotokos as guide unto incorruptible life.
[^7684]: Concluding troparion of Ode 6 of the Theotokos canon.
[^7685]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the Cross Canon meditating on the tomb of Christ through the Three Holy Youths in Babylon (Dan. 3).
[^7686]: Troparion recounting cosmic convulsions at the Crucifixion (Mt. 27:45, 51).
[^7687]: Troparion on the mock coronation of Christ (Mt. 27:29–30), running over onto Leaf p725.
[^7688]: Completion of Troparion 2 of Ode 7 from Leaf p724.
[^7689]: First martyrikon of Ode 7 celebrating the voluntary endurance of the martyrs.
[^7690]: The 1895 Lviv printed edition erroneously prefixes this troparion with the rubric *Бг҃оро́диченъ.* (*Theotokion*), although the hymnographic text is clearly the second martyrikon addressed to the holy martyrs (*Ye stood in the midst of the flame, O all-praised martyrs...*).
[^7691]: Second martyrikon of Ode 7 praising the martyrs who were preserved unconsumed in the furnace like the Three Holy Youths.
[^7692]: True Stavrotheotokion of Ode 7 expressing the anguish of the Theotokos witnessing her Son's voluntary crucifixion.
[^7693]: Rubric indicating the Theotokos canon in Ode 7 repeats the heirmos *Ineffable wonder...*
[^7694]: Troparion petitioning the Mother of God to anchor the soul upon the rock of divine commandments.
[^7695]: Troparion imploring a cleansing shower of penitential tears.
[^7696]: Troparion invoking the protection of the Virgin against demonic snares and the Last Judgment.
[^7697]: Concluding troparion of Ode 7 of the Theotokos canon.
[^7698]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the Cross Canon based on the Song of the Three Holy Children (Dan. 3:52–88). The printed Slavonic witness exhibits an epigraphic dittography (*въ меррвеце́хъ* for *въ мертвеце́хъ*).
[^7699]: Troparion of Ode 8 on Christ tasting the cup of the Passion to redeem man who fell through Eve (the rib of Adam, Gen. 2:21–22), running over onto Leaf p726.
[^7700]: Conclusion of Troparion 1 of Ode 8 from Leaf p725.
[^7701]: Troparion of Ode 8 on the cosmic dread at the crucifixion of the Giver of Life.
[^7702]: First martyrikon of Ode 8 citing Ps. 115:6 ("Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints").
[^7703]: Second martyrikon of Ode 8 citing 2 Cor. 10:4 ("the weapons of our warfare are not carnal").
[^7704]: Stavrotheotokion of Ode 8 uniting the Virgin's lament with the biblical benediction.
[^7705]: Rubric indicating the Theotokos canon in Ode 8 shares the heirmos *Be astonished in fear, O heaven...*
[^7706]: Troparion petitioning the Theotokos for illumination with the uncreated light of Christ.
[^7707]: Troparion petitioning deliverance from afflictions and spiritual despair (Ps. 114:3).
[^7708]: Troparion invoking the protection of the Theotokos against visible and invisible adversaries.
[^7709]: Concluding troparion of Ode 8 of the Theotokos canon.
[^7710]: Rubric for the Magnificat (Lk. 1:46–55) chanted before Ode 9.
[^7711]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Cross Canon (the classical Holy Saturday heirmos *Weep not for Me, O Mother*).
[^7712]: Troparion of Ode 9 contrasting Adam's sweet taste of the tree with Christ's bitter death on the Tree of the Cross.
[^7713]: Troparion contemplating the mysteries of the Passion: the stretch upon the Cross, the crown of thorns, and the vinegar.
[^7714]: First martyrikon of Ode 9 listing the physical torments suffered by the martyrs.
[^7715]: Second martyrikon of Ode 9 comparing the commemoration of the martyrs to solar radiance.
[^7716]: Stavrotheotokion of Ode 9 contemplating the Virgin holding the infant Christ in her arms and beholding Him lifted on the Cross.
[^7717]: Rubric indicating the Theotokos canon in Ode 9 repeats the heirmos *Weep not for Me, O Mother...*
[^7718]: Troparion of personal dedication unto the Mother of God.
[^7719]: Troparion praising the Theotokos as universal shelter, running over onto Leaf p728.
[^7720]: Completion of Troparion 2 of Ode 9 from Leaf p727.
[^7721]: Troparion addressing the Theotokos as firm salvation and vessel of heavenly dew.
[^7722]: Concluding troparion of Ode 9 of the Theotokos canon.
[^7723]: Rubric for the Megalynarion (*It is truly meet*), the Exaposteilarion of Tone 6, and the conclusion of Matins psalmody.
[^7724]: Rubric for the Aposticha stichera at Friday Matins.
[^7725]: Opening Cross sticheron at the Aposticha asserting the divine and human natures of Christ.
[^7726]: First psalm verse at the Aposticha (Ps. 89:14, 16).
[^7727]: Second Cross sticheron at the Aposticha praising the protective power of the Cross against the devil.
[^7728]: Second psalm verse at the Aposticha (Ps. 89:17).
[^7729]: Martyrikon sticheron at the Aposticha celebrating universal cosmic communion between heaven and earth.
[^7730]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha presenting the consolation spoken by the crucified Christ to His Mother.
[^7731]: Rubric indicating the concluding prayers of Friday Matins (Ps. 91:2, Trisagion prayers, troparion, litany, and dismissal).
[^7732]: Heading for the Beatitudes hymns chanted at the Liturgy (or Typika) on Friday.
[^7733]: The Beatitudes refrain (Lk. 23:42).
[^7734]: Troparion on the outstretched hands of Christ embracing all nations upon the Cross.
[^7735]: Troparion on the crushing of the serpent's sting through the Crucifixion, running over onto Leaf p729.
[^7736]: Completion of Troparion 3 of the Friday Beatitudes from Leaf p728.
[^7737]: Martyrikon troparion of the Beatitudes commemorating the martyrs' spiritual joy amidst earthly tortures.
[^7738]: Triadikon of the Beatitudes invoking the consubstantial Trinity.
[^7739]: Stavrotheotokion concluding the Friday Beatitudes.
[^7740]: Primary liturgical heading for Saturday (commemorating All Saints and the Faithful Departed) which begins on Friday evening at Vespers.
[^7741]: Rubric for the beginning of Saturday Vespers served on Friday evening.
[^7742]: Rubric for the first group of stichera at "Lord, I have cried" honoring the three major orders of sanctity (Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Monastics).
[^7743]: Canonical 3-layer melodic model heading for the Tone 6 podoben *Having laid aside all earthly hope* (*Всѐ ѿло́жше* / *Ὅλην ἀποθέμενοι*).
[^7744]: First sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" commemorating the holy martyrs.
[^7745]: Second sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" commemorating the holy hierarchs as faithful shepherds of Christ's flock.
[^7746]: Third sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" commemorating the venerable monastic fathers, continuing across the leaf turn onto Leaf p730.
[^7747]: Completion of Sticheron 3 at "Lord, I have cried" from Leaf p729.
[^7748]: Rubric for the remaining martyric stichera at "Lord, I have cried" on Friday evening, chanted to the same melody (*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope"*).
[^7749]: First martyric sticheron chanted twice.
[^7750]: Second martyric sticheron commemorating the heavenly citizenship of the martyrs.
[^7751]: The solemn Tone 6 Dogmatikon Theotokion (*Кто̀ тебѐ не оу҆блажи́тъ* / *Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε*), confessing the unconfused union of the divine and human natures in the single hypostasis of Christ (Chalcedonian definition).
[^7752]: Rubric for the ancient evening hymn *Phos Hilaron* and the Friday evening prokeimenon (*The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty*, Ps. 92).
[^7753]: Rubric for the Aposticha stichera at Saturday Vespers (sung on Friday evening).
[^7754]: Opening martyric sticheron at the Aposticha.
[^7755]: First psalm verse at the Aposticha (Ps. 122:1–2).
[^7756]: Second martyric sticheron at the Aposticha praising the Cross as the martyrs' unconquerable weapon.
[^7757]: Second psalm verse at the Aposticha (Ps. 122:3–4).
[^7758]: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) (sticheron for the departed) at the Aposticha, meditating on the creation of man from dust and the divine breath (Gen. 2:7) and petitioning eternal rest for the departed.
[^7759]: Concluding Theotokion at the Aposticha invoking the intercession of the All-Holy Virgin and all the ranks of saints, running over onto Leaf p731.
[^7760]: Concluding phrase of the Theotokion at the Aposticha from Leaf p730, completing the petition for the repose of the departed through the intercession of the All-Holy Virgin and all ranks of saints.
[^7761]: Rubric initiating the office of Compline for Friday evening, Tone 6.
[^7762]: Rubric heading for the Supplicatory Canon (Канѡ́нъ моле́бный) addressed to the Most Holy Theotokos at Friday Compline, Tone 6.
[^7763]: Tone 6, Ode 1 Heirmos: "He was my helper and protector unto salvation..." (Exodus 15:1–2).
[^7764]: Refrain for the Canon to the Theotokos: "Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us."
[^7765]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1, pleading that the Virgin not cast away the contrite sinner fleeing beneath her mercy.
[^7766]: Troparion 2 of Ode 1, petitioning deliverance from immeasurable evils and reception of the soul's sighs.
[^7767]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 1, asking deliverance from eternal fire, darkness, and the abyss.
[^7768]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 1, presenting the pure Mother of God as an advocate before Christ the Deliverer.
[^7769]: Ode 3 Heirmos: "Establish, O Lord, upon the rock of Thy commandments..." (1 Sam. / 1 Kgs. 2:2).
[^7770]: Troparion 1 of Ode 3, entreating the Mother of the Word for compassions and forgiveness of sins.
[^7771]: Troparion 2 of Ode 3, praying for deliverance from demonic affliction and everlasting punishment.
[^7772]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 3, asking that the drunkenness of pleasures be subdued by the sobriety of repentance.
[^7773]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 3, beseeching the Parent of God to preserve her flock through her Son and Ruler of all.
[^7774]: Ode 4 Heirmos: "The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid..." (Habakkuk 3:2).
[^7775]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4, lamenting chronic spiritual wounds caused by self-indulgence, running over onto Leaf p732.
[^7776]: Conclusion of Troparion 1 of Ode 4 from Leaf p731, crying for healing and salvation.
[^7777]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4, confessing the darkness of sin and begging the Mother of Divine Light for enlightenment.
[^7778]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 4, confessing the Incarnation of the Word in two natures and one Hypostasis from the pure blood of the Virgin.
[^7779]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 4, expressing contrition over bodily pleasures and pleading for the mercy of the Theotokos.
[^7780]: Ode 5 Heirmos: "Enlighten, I pray, him who rises early from the night..." (Isaiah 26:9).
[^7781]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5, acknowledging self-kindled Gehenna and entreating the Virgin's unfailing help.
[^7782]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5, grieving the Mother of God by repeated sin while beseeching her compassionate cleansing.
[^7783]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 5, confessing Christ born from the Virgin's blood as perfect man and true God, and praying for deliverance from eternal fire.
[^7784]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 5, entreating the Impassable Gate (cf. Ezek. 44:2) to open the pathways of true repentance.
[^7785]: Ode 6 Heirmos: "I cried with all my heart unto the merciful God..." (Jonah 2:2–6).
[^7786]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6, begging the Lady to guide the soul engulfed in wicked thoughts into the calm haven of repentance.
[^7787]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6, praising the Virgin as an unconquerable wall and refuge, running over onto Leaf p733.
[^7788]: Conclusion of Troparion 2 of Ode 6 from Leaf p732, professing deliverance from sins, passions, and harms.
[^7789]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 6, pleading with tears for salvation from future judgment and eternal fire.
[^7790]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 6, appealing to the Virgin as the healer of souls weakened by passions.
[^7791]: Rubric directing the chanting of 'Lord, have mercy' 40 times, followed by 'Glory... both now...'.
[^7792]: Sessional Hymn (Sessional Hymn) at Compline, Tone 6, praising Christ's voluntary descent and Incarnation from the Virgin to elevate humanity unto heaven.
[^7793]: Ode 7 Heirmos: "We have sinned, we have transgressed, we have done unrighteously before Thee..." (Daniel 3:29–35).
[^7794]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7, imploring the Lady's presence and protection from demons at the terrifying hour of the soul's departure from the body.
[^7795]: Troparion 2 of Ode 7, offering tears to the longsuffering and merciful Virgin for pardon and rescue.
[^7796]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 7, asking saving aid against the turmoil and unrest of the flesh.
[^7797]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 7, dogmatic meditation on the co-unoriginate Word assuming rational and ensouled human nature from the Virgin.
[^7798]: Ode 8 Heirmos: "Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and Cherubim and Seraphim tremble before..." (Daniel 3:57).
[^7799]: Troparion 1 of Ode 8, dreading the impending account before the fearful Judge and invoking the Lady's defense, running over onto Leaf p734.
[^7800]: Conclusion of Troparion 1 of Ode 8 from Leaf p733, beseeching the Lady of the world for protection.
[^7801]: Troparion 2 of Ode 8, praying before the Virgin's icon that she may not turn away her face but alter the impending sentence of condemnation.
[^7802]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 8, seeking deliverance from demonic harm and advocacy in the final judgment.
[^7803]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 8, asking that the deifying Incarnation of Christ may grant the cleansed soul participation in His kingdom.
[^7804]: Ode 9 Heirmos: "O Virgin who didst receive from the Angel the Rejoice..." (Luke 1:28).
[^7805]: Troparion 1 of Ode 9, setting forth the Theotokos as the sole protectress and wall of Christians to obtain mercy from Christ.
[^7806]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9, trembling before the night of hades due to deeds of darkness, and imploring rescue from the Mother of the Judge.
[^7807]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 9, acknowledging life spent in evils near the gates of death and placing all hope in the Virgin.
[^7808]: Both now: Troparion 4 of Ode 9, petitioning the Virgin, Angels, and Martyrs for remission of debts and the grace to conclude life righteously.
[^7809]: Concluding rubrics for Compline: 'It is truly meet', Trisagion, Lord's Prayer, troparia, and customary dismissal.
[^7810]: Primary rubric marking the start of Saturday Matins, Tone 6.
[^7811]: Rubric for the Sessional Hymns (Sedalny) to the Holy Martyrs following the First Kathisma reading.
[^7812]: Sessional Hymn 1 to the Martyrs, Tone 6, praising the saints who extinguished the ungodly lamp and shine as luminaries, running over onto Leaf p735.
[^7813]: Conclusion of Sessional Hymn 1 to the Martyrs from Leaf p734, praying for the enlightenment of the soul's lamp.
[^7814]: Psalm verse accompanying the second martyric sessional hymn: Psalm 67:36 [LXX: Ps. 67:36 / MT: Ps. 68:35].
[^7815]: Sessional Hymn 2 to the Martyrs, Tone 6, commemorating their triumph over lawless devices and their incorruptible crowns.
[^7816]: Glory... both now... Theotokion after the First Kathisma, meditating on Christ's voluntary Crucifixion to seek out the lost drachma (Adam, cf. Luke 15:8–9).
[^7817]: Rubric for the Sessional Hymns to the Holy Martyrs following the Second Kathisma reading.
[^7818]: Sessional Hymn 1 to the Martyrs after the Second Kathisma, describing their steadfast endurance before tyrants and the cheering heavenly host.
[^7819]: Psalm verse accompanying the second sessional hymn: Psalm 67:36 [LXX: Ps. 67:36 / MT: Ps. 68:35].
[^7820]: Sessional Hymn 2 to the Martyrs, likening the commemoration of the saints unto the Paradise of Eden.
[^7821]: Psalm verse accompanying the Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) (Departed) sessional hymn: Psalm 33:20 [LXX: Ps. 33:19 / MT: Ps. 34:19].
[^7822]: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) (Sessional Hymn for the Departed), Tone 6, meditating on the vanity of earthly life as shadow and dream (cf. Ps. 143:4) where kings and paupers share the grave.
[^7823]: Canonical 3-layer melodic model rubric: Tone 6, Podoben: "O hope of the world" (Наде́жде мі́ра).
[^7824]: Theotokion to the Podoben, petitioning the Virgin, martyrs, prophets, and ascetics to save the reposed.
[^7825]: Rubric for the First Canon at Saturday Matins, Tone 6, dedicated to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and Departed.
[^7826]: Acrostic declaration and authorship attribution: "With words I honor the lovers of the Word" (Словесы̀ почита́ю люби́телей Сло́ва), composed by Joseph the Hymnographer.
[^7827]: Tone 6, Ode 1 Heirmos: "The sensory Pharaoh was drowned with all his host..." (Exodus 14–15).
[^7828]: Refrain to the Holy Martyrs: "Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us."
[^7829]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1 (First Canon), commemorating the martyrs passing over to the unwaning light.
[^7830]: Refrain to the Hierarchs of Christ: "Hierarchs of Christ, pray to God for us."
[^7831]: Troparion 2 of Ode 1 (First Canon), praising the hierarchs, venerable fathers, prophets, and righteous entering heavenly mansions.
[^7832]: Refrain to All Saints: "All Saints, pray to God for us."
[^7833]: Troparion 3 of Ode 1 (First Canon), honoring the choir of holy women who overcame the deceiver of Eve.
[^7834]: Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) troparion of Ode 1 (First Canon), praying for rest for the departed in the tents of the righteous.
[^7835]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 1 (First Canon), exalting the Virgin as more venerable than Cherubim and Seraphim.
[^7836]: Rubric for the Second Canon at Saturday Matins, Tone 6, dedicated specifically to the Departed.
[^7837]: Acrostic, rubrical directive for performance when no Menaion is appointed, and authorship attribution to Theophanes the Branded: "A sixth song I sing for the departed" (Шесто́е припѣва́ю ѿше́дшымъ пѣ́нїе).
[^7838]: Tone 6, Ode 1 Heirmos (Second Canon): "Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land..." (Exodus 14:29).
[^7839]: Psalm verse: Psalm 67:36 [LXX: Ps. 67:36 / MT: Ps. 68:35].
[^7840]: Troparion 1 of Ode 1 (Second Canon), invoking the noble martyrs to entreat eternal goods for the reposed.
[^7841]: Verse for the departed: "Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants."
[^7842]: Troparion 2 of Ode 1 (Second Canon), meditating on God's fashioning of man as a composite creature of dust and divine breath.
[^7843]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 1 (Second Canon), recalling man's original placement in Paradise, running over onto Leaf p737.
[^7844]: Conclusion of Troparion 3 of Ode 1 (Second Canon) from Leaf p736, meditating on the Fall, exile, and Christ's merciful rest.
[^7845]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 1 (Second Canon), proclaiming the Incarnation of the Creator from the Virgin to undo the curse of Eve and shatter death.
[^7846]: Ode 3 Heirmos (First Canon): "Having established the thoughts of my soul upon the firm rock of Thy faith..." (1 Sam. / 1 Kgs. 2:2).
[^7847]: Troparion 1 of Ode 3 (First Canon), praising the martyrs who soothe human illnesses through grace.
[^7848]: Troparion 2 of Ode 3 (First Canon), commemorating the hierarchs who drove away noetic wolves from Christ's flock.
[^7849]: Troparion 3 of Ode 3 (First Canon), honoring the ascetics who mortified carnal wisdom beneath the Lord's yoke.
[^7850]: Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) troparion of Ode 3 (First Canon), praying for remission of debts and eternal bliss for the pious reposed.
[^7851]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 3 (First Canon), depicting holy women rejoicing around the Mother of God.
[^7852]: Ode 3 Heirmos (Second Canon): "There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God..." (1 Sam. / 1 Kgs. 2:2).
[^7853]: Troparion 1 of Ode 3 (Second Canon), petitioning eternal rest for the departed through the lawful struggles of the martyrs.
[^7854]: Troparion 2 of Ode 3 (Second Canon), recounting God's pedagogical chastisements and Christ's ultimate self-emptying to seek and save the lost.
[^7855]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 3 (Second Canon), praying that the departed may pass from earthly corruption into joy and justification.
[^7856]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 3 (Second Canon), confessing the spotless Mother who conceived the eternal God, running over onto Leaf p738.
[^7857]: Conclusion of Theotokion of Ode 3 (Second Canon) from Leaf p737, declaring the destruction of death's power.
[^7858]: Ode 4 Heirmos (First Canon): "I heard, O Lord, the report of Thee, and was afraid..." (Habakkuk 3:2).
[^7859]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4 (First Canon), portraying the martyrs trampling the proud adversary underfoot.
[^7860]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4 (First Canon), praising the hierarchs whose theological teachings dispelled the darkness of heresy.
[^7861]: Troparion 3 of Ode 4 (First Canon), commemorating the venerable fathers as radiant embers that burned up material passions.
[^7862]: Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) troparion of Ode 4 (First Canon), petitioning Christ the Master of the living and the dead for eternal rest.
[^7863]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 4 (First Canon), honoring the courageous women who followed the incarnate Lord.
[^7864]: Ode 4 Heirmos (Second Canon): "Christ is my power, God and Lord..." (Habakkuk 3:2).
[^7865]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4 (Second Canon), extolling the divine wisdom that joined martyric choirs with the angelic hosts.
[^7866]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4 (Second Canon), praying that the faithful departed may attain Christ's ineffable glory and gladness.
[^7867]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 4 (Second Canon), asking the Merciful Lord to cleanse the sins of the reposed and make them children of light.
[^7868]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 4 (Second Canon), hailing the Virgin as the pure vessel, blameless temple, ark of holiness, and beauty of Jacob (cf. Ps. 46:5 LXX).
[^7869]: Ode 5 Heirmos (First Canon): "O Christ Who hast shone forth light unto the world: enlighten my heart..." (Isaiah 26:9).
[^7870]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5 (First Canon), praising the martyrs who stripped the enemy bare by vesting themselves in grace.
[^7871]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5 (First Canon), honoring prophets, hierarchs, and ascetics, running over onto Leaf p739.
[^7872]: Conclusion of Troparion 2 of Ode 5 (First Canon) from Leaf p738, praising the venerable who pleased God.
[^7873]: Troparion 3 of Ode 5 (First Canon), praising the choir of holy women with psalms and hymns.
[^7874]: Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) troparion of Ode 5 (First Canon), entreating rest for the departed in the tents of the righteous.
[^7875]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 5 (First Canon), invoking the Lady's prayers to Christ to preserve us from demonic injury.
[^7876]: Ode 5 Heirmos (Second Canon): "With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who rise early unto Thee..." (Isaiah 26:9).
[^7877]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5 (Second Canon), praising the martyrs as sacred whole burnt offerings and first-fruits of humanity.
[^7878]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5 (Second Canon), praying that the faithful reposed may receive heavenly dwellings and remission of sins.
[^7879]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 5 (Second Canon), petitioning the immortal Fountain of life to grant His kingdom unto the departed.
[^7880]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 5 (Second Canon), praising the Lord born of the Virgin as salvation from the gates of hades.
[^7881]: Ode 6 Heirmos (First Canon): "Swallowed by the whale of sin, I cry unto Thee, O Christ..." (Jonah 2:1–7).
[^7882]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6 (First Canon), commemorating the martyrs whose blood drowned spiritual foes and refreshed the faithful.
[^7883]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6 (First Canon), praising the ascetics and hierarchs who crucified themselves to the world.
[^7884]: Troparion 3 of Ode 6 (First Canon), blessing the choir of prophets and holy women.
[^7885]: Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) troparion of Ode 6 (First Canon), praying for rest for the departed with the elect.
[^7886]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 6 (First Canon), entreating the Virgin to mortify bodily passions and vivify the soul, running over onto Leaf p740.
[^7887]: Conclusion of Theotokion of Ode 6 (First Canon) from Leaf p739, praying for vivification through the Virgin's advocacy.
[^7888]: Ode 6 Heirmos (Second Canon): "Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations..." (Jonah 2:1–7).
[^7889]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6 (Second Canon), meditating on Christ gathering the martyrs upon the Cross and asking rest for the departed.
[^7890]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6 (Second Canon), praying that the faithful reposed may meet Christ radiantly on the clouds at His glorious Second Coming.
[^7891]: Glory: Troparion 3 of Ode 6 (Second Canon), petitioning the Fountain of life to settle the departed in the delight of Paradise.
[^7892]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 6 (Second Canon), celebrating humanity's elevation from dust to heaven through the Incarnation from the Virgin.
[^7893]: Ode 7 Heirmos (First Canon): "Thou Who didst hear the song of Thy holy Children and didst bedew the burning furnace..." (Daniel 3:26–30).
[^7894]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7 (First Canon), honoring the martyrs who quenched the flames of godlessness by their blood.
[^7895]: Troparion 2 of Ode 7 (First Canon), praising the hierarchs who dispelled the winter of heresy and entered divine springtime.
[^7896]: Troparion 3 of Ode 7 (First Canon), commemorating the fasters whose endurance vanquished hosts of demons.
[^7897]: Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ) troparion of Ode 7 (First Canon), petitioning the Merciful One to grant the departed the haven of divine sweetness.
[^7898]: Both now: Theotokion of Ode 7 (First Canon), praising Christ Who preserved the Virgin's purity and saved the choir of holy women.
[^7899]: Ode 7 Heirmos (Second Canon): "The Angel made the furnace dew-yielding for the holy Children..." (Daniel 3:49–50).
[^7900]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7 (Second Canon), opening commemoration of the martyrs redeemed from Adam's transgression by Christ's blood, running over onto Leaf p741.
